- **AC 2.2.7.1** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `GET /items/export?category={category}&nav={kw1}` or `GET /items/export?category={category}&nav={kw1},{kw2}`, **then** the API returns a JSON array of all visible items in scope (public items + my own private items) formatted in seed-source format (`itemId`, `category`, `navigationKeyword1`, `navigationKeyword2`, `question`, `correctAnswer`, `incorrectAnswers`, `explanation`, `keywords`, `source`). No pagination is applied — all matching items are returned.
- **AC 2.2.7.2** [Anonymous] **Given** I call `GET /items/export` without authentication, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 2.2.7.3** [Authenticated] **Given** `category` is omitted, **then** the API returns 400.
- **AC 2.2.7.4** [Authenticated] **Given** `nav` contains more than two comma-separated keywords, **then** the API returns 400.
- **AC 2.2.7.5** [Authenticated] **Given** navigation keywords are provided, **then** `navigationKeyword1` and `navigationKeyword2` in each exported item are returned as lowercase names matching the seed-source convention.

**UI**

- **AC 2.2.7.6** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated and browsing the Sets view at a nav1 or nav1/nav2 level (e.g. `/categories/languages/bulgarian` or `/categories/languages/bulgarian/idioms`), **then** I see an **Export** button to the left of the **+Add** button in the toolbar (both desktop and mobile).
- **AC 2.2.7.7** [Authenticated] **Given** I click **Export**, **then** the UI fetches all items in the current scope via `GET /items/export`, serialises them as prettified JSON, and triggers a browser file-save dialog with a pre-filled filename of the form `{category}.{kw1}.json` or `{category}.{kw1}.{kw2}.json` (e.g. `languages.bulgarian.idioms.json`). The user may rename the file before saving.
- **AC 2.2.7.8** [Authenticated] **Given** the export is in progress, **then** the Export button is disabled and shows "Exporting…" until the download is triggered.
- **AC 2.2.7.9** [Unauthenticated / root-category level] **Given** I am not authenticated, or I am viewing the root category Sets view with no nav keywords, **then** the Export button is not shown.

---

### AC 2.3 Direct item access by ID

**API**

- **AC 2.3.1** [Owner] **Given** I am the owner of an item, **when** I call `GET /items/{id}`, **then** the API returns the item regardless of `IsPrivate`.
- **AC 2.3.2** [Anyone] **Given** an item exists with `IsPrivate = false`, **when** I call `GET /items/{id}`, **then** the API returns the item (subject to standard status codes for validation or deletion).
- **AC 2.3.3** [Authenticated (non-owner, non-admin) / Anonymous] **Given** an item exists with `IsPrivate = true` and I am not the owner (and not an admin), **when** I call `GET /items/{id}`, **then** the API returns the same 404 (empty body, same headers) as for a non-existent id, so the response does not reveal the item exists (the item is not directly accessible outside collections it belongs to, even if it appears in a collection I can access) (#285).
- **AC 2.3.4** [Anyone] **Given** the item ID does not exist (or has been hard-deleted), **when** I call `GET /items/{id}`, **then** the API returns 404.
- **AC 2.3.6** [Admin (non-owner)] **Given** an item exists with `IsPrivate = true` owned by another user, **when** I call `GET /items/{id}`, **then** the API returns the item (200). Listing and discovery (`GET /items`, review queue, Explore) are unchanged and give admins no extra visibility (#285).

**UI**

- **AC 2.3.5** [Anyone] **Given** I open an item detail page that uses a direct item route (e.g. `/items/{id}`), **when** the page loads, **then** the UI expects the item to be either public or owned by me (the admin Edit flow is the sanctioned exception, AC 2.3.6); items that are only visible through a collection I can access are surfaced via the collection-based routes and APIs (e.g. `/collections/{collectionId}` with `GET /items?collectionId={collectionId}`), not via direct item URLs.

---

### AC 2.4 Item ratings

**API**

- **AC 2.4.1** [Anyone] **Given** I can access the item (accessible by id, or published in a public collection owned by the item's creator), **when** I call `GET /ratings/{itemId}`, **then** the API returns the aggregate rating stats: `count` (number of starred ratings), `averageStars` (rounded to 2 decimal places, or null when no ratings), and the `itemId`. No authentication is required for items accessible to anonymous callers. A missing item, or a private item I cannot access, returns the same empty-body 404 (#293).
- **AC 2.4.2** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `GET /ratings/{itemId}/me`, **then** the API returns my current rating for the item (`id`, `itemId`, `stars`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`), or a null body if I have not yet rated it.
- **AC 2.4.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `GET /ratings/{itemId}/me`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 2.4.4** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `POST /ratings/{itemId}` with `{ "stars": 1..5 }`, **then** the API creates or updates my rating for that item (at most one rating per user per item) and returns 200 with the rating record; repeating the same request updates the stored value. The item must be accessible to me (accessible by id, or published in a public collection owned by the item's creator); otherwise the API returns the same empty-body 404 as a missing id and stores nothing (#293).
- **AC 2.4.5** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `POST /ratings/{itemId}` with `{ "stars": null }`, **then** the API stores a null-stars rating (effectively clearing my vote) but keeps the row; the null stars entry is excluded from `count` and `averageStars`.
- **AC 2.4.6** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `POST /ratings/{itemId}`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 2.4.7** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `POST /ratings/{itemId}` with a `stars` value outside 1–5, **then** the API returns 400 Bad Request.

**UI**

- **AC 2.4.8** [Anyone] **Given** I am viewing an item detail page, **when** the page loads, **then** I see aggregate rating stats (count, average) from `GET /ratings/{itemId}`.
- **AC 2.4.9** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated and viewing an item, **when** the rating component loads, **then** my existing rating is pre-filled (from `GET /ratings/{itemId}/me`); I can click a star (1–5) to create or update my rating via `POST /ratings/{itemId}`; the displayed stats update after the mutation succeeds.
- **AC 2.4.10** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated and viewing an item, **when** I see the rating display, **then** ratings are shown read-only; no rate control is offered.

---

### AC 2.5 Item comments

**API**

- **AC 2.5.1** [Anyone] **Given** I call `GET /comments?itemId={guid}` for an item I can access (accessible by id per AC 2.3.1-2.3.6, or published in a public collection owned by the item's creator), **then** the API returns all comments for that item ordered by creation date (newest first) including `id`, `itemId`, `text`, `createdBy` (user id), `createdByName` (display name), `createdAt`, and `updatedAt`; no authentication is required. **Given** I call `GET /comments` without `itemId` (or with the empty GUID), **then** only comments on items I can access by id (AC 2.3.1-2.3.6) are returned (#285). **Given** the item id does not exist, or the item is private and not accessible to me (including a private item that is only in a private collection or only in another user's public collection), **then** the API returns the same empty-body 404 as a missing id (#293).
- **AC 2.5.2** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `POST /comments` with `{ "itemId": guid, "text": "..." }`, **then** the API creates a comment attached to the item and returns 201 with the created comment; `text` must be non-empty and at most 2000 characters; the item must be accessible to me (accessible by id, or published in a public collection owned by the item's creator); otherwise the API returns the same empty-body 404 as a missing id and creates nothing (#293).
- **AC 2.5.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `POST /comments`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 2.5.4** [Owner of comment] **Given** I am authenticated and I own the comment, **when** I call `PUT /comments/{id}` with `{ "text": "..." }`, **then** the API updates the comment text and sets `updatedAt`, returning 200; `text` must be non-empty and at most 2000 characters.
- **AC 2.5.5** [Authenticated (non-owner)] **Given** I am authenticated but do not own the comment, **when** I call `PUT /comments/{id}`, **then** the API returns 403 Forbidden.
- **AC 2.5.6** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `PUT /comments/{id}`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 2.5.7** [Owner of comment] **Given** I am authenticated and I own the comment, **when** I call `DELETE /comments/{id}`, **then** the API deletes the comment and returns 204 No Content.
- **AC 2.5.8** [Authenticated (non-owner)] **Given** I am authenticated but do not own the comment, **when** I call `DELETE /comments/{id}`, **then** the API returns 403 Forbidden.
- **AC 2.5.9** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `DELETE /comments/{id}`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 2.5.10** [Anyone] **Given** the comment ID does not exist, **when** I call `PUT` or `DELETE /comments/{id}`, **then** the API returns 404.

**UI**

- **AC 2.5.11** [Anyone] **Given** I am viewing an item detail page, **when** the page loads, **then** I can see the comment count for that item and access a comments section (e.g. inline or via navigation to `/items/{id}/comments`).
- **AC 2.5.12** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated and in a study flow (Flashcards or Quiz), **when** I open comments for the current item, **then** the UI opens a **comments drawer** over the current view without navigating away; the drawer shows existing comments, allows me to add a new comment, and lets me edit or delete my own comments; closing the drawer returns me to my previous position in the study flow.
- **AC 2.5.13** [Owner of comment] **Given** I own a comment, **when** I view it in the comments section or drawer, **then** I see edit and delete controls for that comment; editing opens an inline editor; deleting removes the comment from the list immediately.
- **AC 2.5.14** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I view the comments section, **then** I can read existing comments but the add-comment control is not offered (or is disabled with a sign-in prompt).

### AC 2.6 Upload items to collection

This flow lets authenticated users create items in bulk by pasting AI-generated JSON and automatically organizes them into a new private collection.

**API**

- **AC 2.6.1** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `POST /items/upload-to-collection` with `category` (required), `keyword1` (rank-1, required), `keyword2` (rank-2, required), `keywords` (optional extra keywords list), `items` (array, at least one, max 100 for regular users or 1000 for admins), and `inputText` (raw upload content used for duplicate detection), **then** the API creates the items as private (`IsPrivate = true` for non-admins), creates a new collection with an auto-generated GUID name, adds all successfully created items to that collection, and returns 201 with `collectionId`, `name`, and `itemCount`; validation errors return 400.
- **AC 2.6.2** [Authenticated] **Given** the SHA-256 hash of `inputText` matches an existing upload record for my user, **when** I call `POST /items/upload-to-collection`, **then** the API returns a conflict error (duplicate upload detection); the same JSON content cannot be uploaded twice by the same user.
- **AC 2.6.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `POST /items/upload-to-collection`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 2.6.4** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `POST /items/upload-to-collection`, **then** items are created as public (`IsPrivate = false`) instead of private.

**UI**

- **AC 2.6.5** [Authenticated] **Given** I am on the import page at `/create/import` (legacy `/items/upload`, which redirects), **when** the page loads, **then** I see: (1) category, rank-1, and rank-2 dropdowns pre-filled from URL params where available; (2) an example AI prompt I can copy and paste into an AI assistant; (3) a text area to paste the AI JSON response or a file upload control to load a JSON file; and (4) a content-compliance warning (see AC 4.7.10).
- **AC 2.6.6** [Authenticated] **Given** I paste valid JSON and select category/rank-1/rank-2, **when** I submit, **then** the UI calls `POST /items/upload-to-collection`; on success it navigates me to the new collection in Flashcards mode (`/explore/collections/{id}`).
- **AC 2.6.7** [Authenticated] **Given** I submit a duplicate upload (same JSON already uploaded before), **when** the API returns a conflict, **then** the UI shows an error explaining that this content was already uploaded.
- **AC 2.6.8** [Authenticated] **Given** I am on the Upload to Collection page, **when** I read the example prompt, **then** it asks for 1-4 `incorrectAnswers`; incorrect answers are trimmed, blanks dropped and at most 4 kept, and if any row has no non-blank incorrect answer, submitting shows a row-numbered inline error ("Items 2, 3: provide at least one non-blank incorrect answer (1-4).") and makes no API call (#250). If any row has incorrect answers that repeat each other or equal the correct answer (case-insensitive, trimmed), submitting shows a row-numbered inline error ("Items 2, 3: incorrect answers must be different from each other and from the correct answer.") and makes no API call (#276).

- **AC 2.6.9** [Authenticated] **Given** I am on the Upload to Collection page, **when** I open the **Paste text** tab (the other tab is JSON), **then** I can paste a Quizlet export (term TAB definition per line), an Anki text export, CSV or TSV and see a live preview before anything is uploaded (#257). Category, rank-1 and rank-2 are the same required fields as for JSON; the upload calls the same `POST /items/upload-to-collection` and navigates to the new collection on success. Parsing is done in the browser:
  - Separator: auto-detect (an Anki `#separator:` line, else tab if the first row has one, else comma), or tab, comma, semicolon, pipe, or a custom text (may be several characters). Double quotes act as CSV field quotes (embedded separators, line breaks and `""` escapes) unless that checkbox is cleared; a field is quoted only when its closing quote is followed by a separator, a line break or the end, so `"To be" - who?` or an unbalanced `"` stays literal and never swallows later rows. The two tabs (JSON, Paste text) are keyboard operable with the arrow keys, Home and End. Only the first two columns are used; a header row written by the collection export (**AC 1.14.2**) is skipped.
  - Anki: header lines for Anki's known keys (`#separator`, `#html`, `#columns`, `#tags column`, `#notetype`, `#deck`, `#guid column`...) are ignored only before the first data row, so a row such as `#Note: hi` is kept; the `#separator:` line also chooses the separator; HTML tags are removed and entities such as `&nbsp;` and `&amp;` are decoded. Blank lines and rows missing a term or a definition are skipped and listed in the preview.
  - Direction: term as question and definition as answer, or the reverse. An optional question template such as `Define: {term}` (`{term}` is the question side; a template without it is used as a prefix).
  - Wrong answers come from the same set with no AI: up to 3 other distinct answers, chosen deterministically (the same text always gives the same items), never equal (case-insensitive, trimmed) to the correct answer or to another answer for the same question, so every item meets **AC 2.1.7** (1-4 distinct, non-blank incorrect answers different from the correct answer). At least 4 usable rows are needed, otherwise the preview explains why and the upload button is disabled. A row with no other distinct answer is skipped.
  - Rows whose answer is over 200 characters or whose question is over 1,000 characters are skipped and listed. Identical question and answer pairs (case-insensitive) in the pasted text are imported once.
  - The batch is limited to 100 items (1,000 for admins), as in **AC 2.6.1**: when the text has more, the preview warns that only the first 100 will be imported and the rest must be uploaded separately. Duplicate-upload detection (**AC 2.6.2**) uses the generated items, so uploading the same resulting items twice is rejected.
  - Quizlet links are never fetched or scraped, and Anki `.apkg` files are not supported.

### AC 2.7 Admin: set item visibility

**API**

- **AC 2.7.1** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `PUT /items/{id}/visibility` with `{ "isPrivate": true|false }`, **then** the API updates the item's `IsPrivate` flag and returns 200 with `id` and `isPrivate`; this allows admins to promote or demote any item between private and public without going through the review board flow.
- **AC 2.7.2** [Authenticated (non-admin)] **Given** I am authenticated but not an admin, **when** I call `PUT /items/{id}/visibility`, **then** the API returns 403 Forbidden.
- **AC 2.7.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `PUT /items/{id}/visibility`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 2.7.4** [Admin] **Given** the item does not exist, **when** I call `PUT /items/{id}/visibility`, **then** the API returns 404 Not Found.

### AC 2.8 Admin review board for items

**API**

- **AC 2.8.1** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/items/review-board`, **then** the API returns only items where the internal flag `ReadyForReview = true`, ordered by creation date (newest first), including question, answers, category, source, factual risk (if any), and review comments (if any).
- **AC 2.8.2** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `PUT /admin/items/{id}/approval` for an item that is ready for review, **then** the API makes the item **public** (`IsPrivate = false`) and clears the review flag (`ReadyForReview = false`) so it no longer appears in the review board.
- **AC 2.8.3** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `PUT /admin/items/{id}/rejection` with an optional `reason` in the body for an item that is ready for review, **then** the API keeps the item **private** (`IsPrivate = true`), clears the review flag (`ReadyForReview = false`), and appends a rejection note to `ReviewComments` including the rejection time, the admin identifier, and the optional reason text.

**UI**

- **AC 2.8.4** [Admin] **Given** I am on the Admin review board screen for items, **when** the list loads, **then** I see only items that are marked ready for review (per API), with enough context to decide (question, answers, category, source, factual risk, comments); from this screen I can **approve** an item, which makes it public and removes it from the list, or **reject** an item with an optional free-text reason, which keeps it private, removes it from the list, and stores the rejection note in the item's review comments.

### AC 2.8a Admin review board for keywords

**API**

- **AC 2.8a.1** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/keywords/pending`, **then** the API returns only private, review-pending keywords that are attached to at least one item, ordered by creation date (oldest first), including name, slug, creator, created time, and usage count.
- **AC 2.8a.2** [Admin] **Given** I approve a private review-pending keyword via `POST /admin/keywords/{id}/approve`, **when** a public keyword with the same canonical name already exists, **then** the API merges item-keyword links onto the existing public keyword, removes the duplicate private pending row, records review metadata on the surviving public keyword, and returns the surviving keyword in the response instead of failing with a duplicate-key error.
- **AC 2.8a.3** [Admin] **Given** I approve a private review-pending keyword via `POST /admin/keywords/{id}/approve`, **when** no matching public keyword exists, **then** the API makes that keyword public, clears the review-pending flag, records review metadata, and removes it from the pending list.
- **AC 2.8a.4** [Admin] **Given** I reject a private review-pending keyword via `POST /admin/keywords/{id}/reject`, **then** the API keeps the keyword private, clears the review-pending flag, records review metadata, and removes it from the pending list.

**UI**

- **AC 2.8a.5** [Admin] **Given** I am on `/admin/keyword-review`, **when** I approve or reject a keyword successfully, **then** that keyword is removed from the visible list immediately and the page refreshes the pending-keyword query in the background.
- **AC 2.8a.6** [Admin] **Given** a keyword approve or reject request fails, **when** I remain on `/admin/keyword-review`, **then** the UI shows an inline error message with the API failure detail instead of failing silently.

## 3. Categories and keywords

Categories are **public only** (there is no private category). Users navigate by category and by **keywords** (e.g. primary topic, subtopic). The same item set can be viewed in **Sets**, **List**, **Flashcards**, or **Quiz** mode — similar to collection views; the collection view does not have a "Sets" mode. Navigation and all views under Categories (overview, sets, list, flashcards, quiz) are described in this section.

**URL and slugs:** Category and navigation keywords appear in the UI URL. **Slugs** are used for the category segment **and** for navigation keywords: names are converted to a URL-friendly slug (lowercase, spaces to dashes, special characters removed; e.g. "ACT Math" → `act-math`, "World Records" → `world-records`). The frontend resolves the category slug back to the canonical category name (e.g. from `GET /categories`) for API calls. Keyword segments in the path use keyword **slugs**; the backend resolves those slugs to canonical keyword names and IDs. **Navigation keywords** can appear (1) in the **route** as path segments, e.g. `/categories/science/world-records`, and (2) in **query** parameters, e.g. `?keywords=s3,ec2`. The same keyword can appear in both route and query; route defines the navigation scope (e.g. category + primary topic), and query adds extra filter (AND semantics). Items returned are those matching the combined scope.

**Actors:** **Anonymous**, **Authenticated** (may see additional private keywords and private items they created), **Admin**. There is no "category owner"; only **Admin** can create/update/delete categories and keyword relations (navigation structure).

### AC 3.1 Listing categories

**API**

- **AC 3.1.1** [Anyone] **Given** categories exist, **when** I call `GET /categories` with optional `search`, **then** the API returns all **public** categories (name, description, shortDescription, count, averageStars) matching the search on category name; item counts and average ratings consider only items I am allowed to see (anonymous: non-private items; authenticated: non-private items plus my private items). There are no private categories.
- **AC 3.1.2** [Anyone] **Given** I call `GET /categories`, **then** the API returns 200 with a list of categories; no authentication is required.

**UI**

- **AC 3.1.3** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the Categories page at `/categories`, **when** the list loads, **then** I see all public categories per API; I can search by name and sort by name, number of items, or average rating; categories are paginated (e.g. 30 per page) and clicking a category opens the Sets view for that category (e.g. `/categories/{slug}`).

### AC 3.2 Category slugs and resolving category from URL

**API**

- **AC 3.2.1** [Anyone] **Given** a category name, **when** the frontend builds a category URL segment, **then** the category is represented by a **slug** (lowercase, spaces to dashes, special characters removed); the API accepts category **name** (e.g. from `GET /categories` or from slug resolution). Resolution from slug to name is done by the frontend by matching the slug against category names (e.g. `categoryNameToSlug(name) === slug`).

**UI**

- **AC 3.2.2** [Anyone] **Given** I open a URL like `/categories/act-math` or `/categories/science/biology`, **when** the page loads, **then** the first path segment after `/categories/` is the category slug; the UI resolves it to the canonical category name and uses that name for all API calls (e.g. `GET /keywords?category=ACT Math`). If the slug does not match any category, the UI shows "Category not found" and a link back to Categories.

---

### AC 3.3 Navigation keywords (route and query)

**What are navigation keywords?** They are **keywords** (from the Keywords table) linked per category in the **KeywordRelation** table. Each row has: CategoryId, **ParentKeywordId** (null = root/rank-1), **ChildKeywordId**, SortOrder, Description. **Rank-1** = relations with ParentKeywordId null (e.g. "aws", "azure" under category "Certs"). **Rank-2** = relations with ParentKeywordId set to a rank-1 keyword ID (e.g. "Solutions Architect Associate" under "aws"); the same child keyword can appear under multiple parents in the same category. Keywords that are not a child in any KeywordRelation for that category are **item-level** (tags on items only); they can still be used as filters. Each **item** has required **NavigationKeywordId1** (rank-1) and **NavigationKeywordId2** (rank-2). The keyword **name** comes from the Keywords table and is what appears in API parameters; the URL path uses the **keyword slug**, which the backend resolves to the canonical name and ID.

**Route vs query:** Navigation keywords (and optionally item-level keywords) can appear in the **route** as path segments (keyword slugs, e.g. `/categories/certs/aws`) and/or in **query** parameters (e.g. `?keywords=s3,ec2`). Path keywords define the **navigation scope** (category + rank-1, optionally rank-2). Query keywords add an extra **filter** (items must have those keywords too; AND semantics). The same keyword may appear in both route and query; the effective scope is the combination. Everyone can use **public** keywords (Keywords.IsPrivate = false). Authenticated users may also see and use **private** keywords they created (Keywords.IsPrivate = true, CreatedBy = user); when a public and a private keyword share the same slug/name, the **public** keyword wins and the private one is only used when no public match exists.

**API**

- **AC 3.3.1** [Anyone] **Given** I call `GET /keywords?category={categoryName}` with optional `selectedKeywords` (comma-separated), **then** the API returns the next navigation layer (rank-1 or rank-2 keywords) for that category, with item counts and average ratings; only **public** keywords are returned to anonymous users; authenticated users get public keywords plus their own **private** keywords.
- **AC 3.3.1b** [Anyone] **Given** I call `GET /keywords/item-tags?category={categoryName}`, **then** the API returns sorted distinct keyword names that appear as item-level tags (`ItemKeywords`) on at least one item in that category that is visible to me, and only keyword names I am allowed to see (public keywords, or my private keywords); **404** if the category does not exist or is not accessible. The web app caches this list per category for create/edit item autocomplete (prefix match, limited rows in the dropdown).
- **AC 3.3.2** [Anyone] **Given** I call `GET /items` with `category` and optional `keywords` (comma-separated), **then** the API returns items in that category that have **all** specified keywords (AND semantics); item visibility applies (anonymous: non-private; authenticated: non-private + own private). Keywords in the request can be navigation (rank-1/rank-2) or item-level; same semantics whether the UI sent them from route or query.
- **AC 3.3.3** [Anyone] **Given** a category has a special **"other"** keyword (rank-1), **when** I request keywords or items with keyword "other", **then** "other" represents items in that category whose **NavigationKeywordId1** is null or not in the set of root child keyword IDs for that category; the UI may display it as "Others". "Other" cannot be combined with other keywords in the same scope.

**UI**

- **AC 3.3.4** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the Categories flow, **when** I navigate by category and keywords, **then** the UI URL may include navigation keywords in the **path** (e.g. `/categories/science/biology`) and/or in the **query** (e.g. `?keywords=s3,ec2`); path defines the Sets hierarchy and scope; query adds filter; the same keyword can appear in both. Links and API calls use the resolved category name and the combined keyword set (path + query, deduped).

---

### AC 3.4 Sets view (category + keywords)

**API**

- **AC 3.4.1** [Anyone] **Given** I have a category name and optional selected keywords (path or query), **when** I call `GET /keywords?category={name}&selectedKeywords=...`, **then** the API returns the next level of buckets (rank-1 or rank-2 navigation keywords, or at leaf item-level keywords) with counts and ratings; invalid or inaccessible paths are handled per keyword visibility (e.g. 404 if category not found).

**UI**

- **AC 3.4.2** [Anyone] **Given** I am on a category Sets view (e.g. `/categories/{slug}` or `/categories/{slug}/{kw1}/{kw2}`), **when** the page loads, **then** I see a grid of buckets (keywords or sub-keywords) with names, item counts, and optional descriptions; clicking a bucket either navigates deeper (adds keyword to path) or, at leaf, opens the **List** view for that scope. I see a breadcrumb (Categories → category → keyword(s)) and can switch to **List**, **Flashcards**, or **Quiz** for the same scope. [Authenticated] **Given** I am signed in, **when** I am on that Sets view, **then** I see an **Add** control on the **right side of the scope secondary bar** (same row as **Sets | List | ...**) that navigates to `/create` with `category` and `keywords` pre-filled from the current navigation path and any `keywords` query filters (path first, then query extras, deduped case-insensitively).
- **AC 3.4.3** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the Sets view, **when** I use the filter panel and select **"All categories"**, **then** the scope becomes all categories (e.g. slug `all` or `/categories` with view); I can still apply keyword filters (e.g. via query) and see items across all categories that match.
- **AC 3.4.4** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the Sets view and have reached the end of the sets hierarchy (no more buckets; message e.g. "You've reached the end of the sets hierarchy"), **when** the page is shown, **then** leaf scopes follow **AC 3.4.4a**; otherwise the scope secondary bar still shows **Sets | List | Flashcards | Quiz** with **Sets** selected.
- **AC 3.4.4a** [Anyone] **Given** I open a leaf category scope that has no child buckets (for example a category plus rank-2 keyword such as `/categories/exams/act/math`, or any scope whose next-level buckets are empty), **when** the page loads, **then** the UI defaults to **List** for that scope instead of showing an empty Sets screen, and the scope secondary bar omits **Sets** and shows only **List | Flashcards | Quiz**.
- **AC 3.4.4b** [Anyone] **Given** I am on a non-leaf category Sets view, **when** the page is shown, **then** the breadcrumb row at the top shows the current scope path with the current scope item count in parentheses, and any short guidance is shown as a compact hint rather than as a separate large heading block.

---

### AC 3.5 List view (items in category/keywords scope)

**API**

- **AC 3.5.1** [Anyone] **Given** I call `GET /items?category={name}&keywords=...` with pagination, **then** the API returns items in that category that have all given keywords; item visibility and pagination apply; no authentication required for public items.

**UI**

- **AC 3.5.2** [Anyone] **Given** I am viewing items in category/keywords scope (**List** mode, e.g. `view=items` on the category path), **when** the list loads, **then** I see the same scope as in Sets (category + path keywords + query keywords).
- **AC 3.5.3** [Anyone] **Given** I am in **List** mode (category/keywords scope), **when** I interact with the list, **then** I can change page size, paginate, and use scope filters (e.g. item type, search, rating).
- **AC 3.5.4** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated and viewing items in category/keywords scope (**List** mode), **when** the list loads, **then** I see add/remove-from-collection and active-collection controls per AC 1.2.5–1.2.8.
- **AC 3.5.5** [Anyone] **Given** I am in **List** mode (category/keywords scope), **when** the list is shown, **then** the breadcrumb displays the path (e.g. Categories → category → keyword(s)) with the **item count** in parentheses after it (e.g. "Categories > language > french (5)"); the UI does **not** show a separate "Items" heading or "Items in ..." paragraph below the breadcrumb.

---

- **AC 3.5.6** [Anyone] **Given** I open an item's details screen from category/global **List** mode, **when** I use the back control on the item-details screen, **then** I return to the same list URL I came from, including the current category path and active query-string filters.

### AC 3.6 Flashcards and Quiz by category/keywords

**API**

- **AC 3.6.1** [Anyone] **Given** I request items by category and keywords (e.g. `GET /items?category=...&keywords=...`), **when** I use those items in Flashcards or Quiz, **then** the same item set and visibility rules apply; no separate category-specific explore/quiz endpoint is required.

**UI**

- **AC 3.6.2** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the category Sets or **List** view, **when** I switch to **Flashcards** or **Quiz**, **then** I am taken to Flashcards or Quiz for the **current category/keyword scope** (e.g. `/explore/{categorySlug}` or `/explore/{categorySlug}/item/{itemId}` with same scope); the set of items is the same as in **List** view for that scope. Behavior (one item at a time, navigation, scoring) is the same as for collection-based Flashcards/Quiz where applicable.
- **AC 3.6.2a** [Anyone] **Given** I am in **Flashcards** mode for a **category/keywords** or **global** scope, **when** I view the current item, **then** the **question** is shown face-up on the card; **when** I click the card, **then** it transitions to show the **correct answer** and **explanation** (if any); **when** I click the answer section, **then** I can flip back to the question; **when** I go to the previous or next item, **then** the card resets to show the question face-up. If the explanation exceeds ~220 characters it is initially truncated with a **Show more** toggle that expands the explanation in place without flipping the card; clicking **Show less** collapses it again.
- **AC 3.6.3** [Anyone] **Given** I am on **Flashcards** or **Quiz** for a **category** scope, **when** I view the scope secondary bar, **then** non-leaf scopes show **Sets | List | Flashcards | Quiz | Learn** (same as on Sets/**List**), and clicking **Sets** navigates back to the Sets view for that scope. Leaf scopes follow **AC 3.6.3a**. For **collection** scope, the bar shows only **List | Flashcards | Quiz | Learn** (no Sets).
- **AC 3.6.3a** [Anyone] **Given** I am on **Flashcards** or **Quiz** for a **leaf category** scope, **when** I view the scope secondary bar, **then** it omits **Sets** and shows only **List | Flashcards | Quiz | Learn**.
- **AC 3.6.4** [Anyone] **Given** I am on **Flashcards** or **Quiz** for a category scope, **when** the page is shown, **then** I see the same breadcrumb and item count at the top as in **List** mode (for example `Categories > exams > act > math (3)`), and the mode guidance is shown as a compact hint instead of a large page title plus descriptive paragraph.
- **AC 3.6.5** [Anyone] **Given** I am in **Quiz** mode for a **category or global** scope (not a collection) and I change **Quiz size**, **when** the new size is applied (on leaving the Quiz size field), **then** the app requests a **new random set** of that many items for the current scope, clears in-session cached item lists used for quiz navigation, resets quiz progress for that run, and updates the URL so it no longer points at an item from the previous set unless that item is re-selected by the new load.
- **AC 3.6.5a** [Anyone] **Given** I am in **Quiz** mode, **when** the quiz card is visible, **then** the **Quiz size** input, **item counter** (`X of Z`), and **item-details eye icon** all appear on a single compact stripe above the question, in the layout `{Quiz size} | {< X of Z >} | {👁}`, so that the question, answer options, score, and the Previous/Next buttons are all visible on a standard laptop screen at 100% zoom without scrolling.

---

- **AC 3.6.6** [Anyone] **Given** I open an item's details screen from category/global Flashcards or Quiz mode, **when** I use the back control on the item-details screen, **then** I return to the same Flashcards or Quiz URL I came from (including the current item and query-string scope); for Quiz this includes restoring the current item's revealed / answered state when that session state is still available.
- **AC 3.6.7** [Anyone] **Given** a category, global, or collection quiz's intended item scope has loaded, **when** a run starts, **then** the app freezes an ordered, duplicate-free roster identified by item ID and creates one run ID for that roster and normalized scope. The run ID and roster remain stable across item URL navigation, query refreshes, comments/details round trips, browser history, and valid restoration. Changing the scope or category/global quiz size creates a new run.
- **AC 3.6.8** [Anyone] **Given** I submit an answer in a quiz run, **when** the answer is accepted, **then** in Practice mode only the first submitted answer for that item counts and it remains locked for that run. Revisiting the item restores the selected answer and feedback. Answered count, correct count, missed item IDs, and rounded percentage are derived from the roster's per-item outcomes rather than independently incremented totals, including when questions are answered out of order. In Test mode (AC 3.18), answers stay changeable and show no feedback until submit; the final answer counts.
- **AC 3.6.9** [Anyone] **Given** in Practice mode the nonempty run roster has an outcome for every item, **when** the final unanswered item is submitted in any roster position, **then** the final answer feedback and explanation remain visible, completion is announced once, and **View results** is exposed. Explicitly entering results moves focus to the **Quiz complete** heading and shows correct/total, rounded percentage, missed count, **Review mistakes** only when applicable, **Play again**, and a scope-appropriate browse/return action. Loading, failed, and empty rosters never complete. A Test (AC 3.18) completes only on submit or timeout, never on the last answer, with the heading **Test complete**.
- **AC 3.6.10** [Anyone] **Given** a completed run has missed answers, **when** I choose **Review mistakes**, **then** I see a read-only review in original roster order with my original response, the correct answer, and available explanation, plus **Back to results**. Review does not change the score or produce another completion. If completed-run content later becomes unavailable, the historical score remains unchanged and the review explains unavailable missed questions without restoring unauthorized content. In Test, Review mistakes also lists unanswered questions, marked "Not answered" (AC 3.18.8).
- **AC 3.6.11** [Anyone] **Given** I choose **Play again** from results, **when** replay begins, **then** the same loaded roster starts with a new run ID and cleared outcomes, and answer options may be reshuffled once for the new run. Requesting a new random set remains the separate size/scope action in **AC 3.6.5**. In Test, Play again keeps the roster and time limit and returns to the setup card (AC 3.18.10).
- **AC 3.6.12** [Anyone] **Given** quiz state is restored, **when** the snapshot schema (version 3, or a version 2 snapshot that predates per-answer `inputMode` and restores its answers as multiple choice; version 1 and unknown versions are rejected), normalized scope, exact ordered roster, and anonymous/authenticated identity match currently permitted API results, **then** the run and its locked outcomes can resume. Aggregate-only legacy state, mismatched identity/scope/roster, deleted or inaccessible active-run items, and invalid snapshots start safely without fabricated results; a stored answer must carry a valid `inputMode` (`choice` or `typed`, **AC 3.6.19**), and a Test snapshot may not contain typed answers; storage failures do not block in-memory play. A completed run exposes one immutable completion snapshot containing only run ID, normalized scope, completion timestamp, correct/total, and missed item IDs; it contains no question text, answer text, or tokens and is not emitted again by review or restoration. Learn progress (AC 3.17.7, 3.17.8) is the exception: it lives in localStorage `quizymode.learn.v1`, and only guest category and global runs keep a question and answer snapshot. The snapshot schema is version 2: it adds the run mode (part of restore identity) and the Test fields (flags, start, limit, submit time). Version 1 snapshots and inconsistent Test fields start a new run (AC 3.18.7).
- **AC 3.6.13** [Anonymous] **Given** a quiz run's completion snapshot (**AC 3.6.12**) is published, **when** the run was started and completed anonymously with auth state settled, **then** a minimal summary is upserted by run ID into the browser-local `localStorage` document `quizymode.guest.v1` (schema version 2): run ID (at most 128 characters), completion timestamp, integer correct/total (`0 <= correct <= total <= 1000`, `total > 0`), a structured source (public collection GUID; or category slug plus canonical 30-character navigation/keyword slugs; or global keywords; non-canonical keyword lists are dropped), and deduplicated missed item GUIDs (none for a perfect run), and the study `mode` (`quiz` or `learn`; Learn writes `learn`, Quiz `quiz`). No question/answer text, selected answers, URLs, query strings, free-text search, emails, tokens, or new visitor identifier are stored. Restoring, remounting, or reviewing the same completed run writes no duplicate; **Play again** adds one new result. Authenticated runs and runs that span an identity change are never written. Before each write the stored document is reread and merged by run ID (best-effort across tabs; a simultaneous two-tab write may lose one result, by design there is no locking). Retention: newest 50 summaries; summaries older than 90 days or with invalid/future timestamps are discarded on read and before write; at most 200 missed-item references are kept across summaries, trimming the oldest results' references first without changing their scores. A stored version 1 document (written before the mode existed) is still read, with every entry treated as a `quiz` run, and is rewritten as version 2 on the next write; an unknown or missing mode on an entry is read as `quiz`; other versions are discarded. Storage, JSON, quota, or removal failures never block gameplay; an unsaved result is kept in memory for the current page session.
- **AC 3.6.14** [Authenticated] **Given** I complete a quiz run that I started and completed while signed in under the same identity (**AC 3.6.12**), **when** the completion snapshot is published, **then** the SPA saves it with `POST /api/study-sessions` (run ID, completion timestamp, correct/total, structured source as in **AC 3.6.13**, missed item GUIDs, and an optional `mode` of `quiz` (default when omitted, so older clients keep working) or `learn`; no question or answer text, selected answers, URLs, or tokens). Any other mode value returns 400. The stored and returned session includes its `mode`; rows created before the mode existed read as `quiz`. The call is idempotent by (user, run ID): a retry or replay of the same run returns the stored session with `created=false` (200) and never overwrites the first stored score; a new run returns 201 with `created=true`. Validation (400): run ID 1-128 characters of letters, digits, or hyphen; `1 <= total <= 1000`; `0 <= correct <= total`; completion time not more than 5 minutes in the future and not older than 365 days; source kind `collection`, `category`, or `global` with canonical slugs (at most 10 keywords of at most 30 characters); missed item IDs unique, at most 200, and at most `total - correct`. Anonymous callers receive 401. The SPA retries transient failures up to twice with the same run ID; a failure never blocks gameplay or results. One row is kept per completed run (not per answer attempt) and changed answers keep no audit trail, since only the first answer per item is scored (Practice) or the final answer at submit (Test) (**AC 5.5.2**). Each session has an `origin`: `quiz` for live runs, `guest-import` reserved for unverified browser-local history. Sessions older than 365 days are deleted when the same user saves a later run. History is private to the account: there is no public comparison, leaderboard, or cross-user read. The misses of a newly saved live run also enter my review queue (AC 3.14.1); guest imports (AC 3.6.16) do not.
- **AC 3.6.15** [Authenticated] **Given** I have saved study sessions, **when** I call `GET /api/study-sessions?limit=N` (default 20, maximum 50) or `GET /api/study-sessions/summary`, **then** I receive only my own sessions newest first, or my totals (sessions, questions, correct, accuracy percent, last-30-day sessions and accuracy percent, count of imported sessions); accuracy is null when there are no questions. Anonymous callers receive 401.
- **AC 3.6.16** [Authenticated] **Given** I sign in or sign up in a browser holding guest history (**AC 3.6.13**), **when** the app loads with settled auth, **then** the SPA automatically (no confirmation step) sends the retained guest summaries once per page session to `POST /api/study-sessions/import` as a versioned payload `{ version: 1, runs: [...] }` (1-50 runs, same fields as **AC 3.6.14** including the optional per-run `mode`, which is stored and defaults to `quiz` when absent; the payload `version` stays 1; other versions or sizes return 400). Each run is validated like a live save; invalid runs are reported in `rejectedRunIds` and discarded, valid ones are stored with origin `guest-import` (unverified, never presented as verified) and counted in the summary's `importedSessions`. Import is idempotent by (user, run ID): runs already stored (live or imported) are counted as `duplicates` and never overwritten, and a repeated run ID inside one payload counts once. Temporary bookmarks and any other guest state are not created. Guest storage is cleared only after the server confirms the whole import (imported, duplicate, or rejected for every run); on any network or server failure it is kept and an alert offers **Retry** (the import also retries on the next page session). On success the SPA shows a dismissible status **"Saved N quiz results from this browser to your account"** noting they are imported and unverified.
- **AC 3.6.17** [Anyone] **Given** quiz rosters and options are built, **then** an item is quiz-playable when at least one incorrect answer remains after trimming, dropping blanks, dropping case-insensitive duplicates and dropping values equal to the correct answer. Quiz options never show blank, duplicate or correct-answer-equivalent distractors (at most 3 are shown). Category, global and collection Quiz rosters, including restore validation (**AC 3.6.12**, **AC 1.5.9**), leave out unplayable items; a deep link to an unplayable item never shows that item: the quiz starts on the remaining playable items of the roster, or shows the existing empty state when none is playable (the URL is left unchanged). A cold quiz deep link (`/quiz/item/{id}` or `/quiz/{category}/item/{id}` with no saved run for that scope in this tab) starts the run on its entry item (the item named by the link when the page was opened) when that item is quiz-playable, is not a private item, and is in the quiz's scope. The entry item comes first, followed by the random items without duplicates, and the run keeps the quiz size. In scope means any item I can read for a global quiz, or an item of the URL's category for a category quiz. When the link carries `nav` or `keywords` filters, the entry item is not added and the rule below applies. The roster is built only after the category name and the random items have loaded (a failed category request stops the wait), so a cold category link never starts a one-item run that is then replaced (no "Some questions from the saved run are no longer available" notice), and moving between questions never changes the roster (#278). When a quiz deep link (`/quiz/item/{id}`, `/quiz/{category}/item/{id}`, `/quiz/collections/{collectionId}[/{slug}]/item/{id}`) names an item that is unplayable, out of scope, or outside a saved or collection roster, the page replaces the URL, without adding a history entry, with the item actually shown, so the URL always names the item shown; the quiz's scope and return query parameters are kept, as Prev/Next keep them, and in that case slugless or wrong-slug collection links get the collection's canonical slug once its details have loaded. For collection quizzes, reloading therefore shows the same item (#275). The Daily quiz selection (**AC 3.13.2**) never picks an unplayable item (the client does not filter the Daily roster, so its total matches the server set), and the review queue (**AC 3.14.2**) never serves or counts one. Flashcards still show unplayable items. The same filter is implemented in `quizRun.ts` (`isQuizPlayable`/`getQuizOptions`) and server-side in `QuizPlayability`. Item writes reject incorrect-answer lists with duplicates or a value equal to the correct answer (**AC 2.1.7**), so only legacy items can be unplayable for that reason. The server compares with ordinal case-insensitive matching on the stored text (markup such as `{{lang|text||pron}}` included) and the web with trim and lower-casing, so rare Unicode edge cases can differ; the server is authoritative (#276).
- **AC 3.6.18** [Anyone] **Given** I open a cold Flashcards deep link (`/explore/item/{id}` or `/explore/{category}/item/{id}`, no stored list and no collection) to a public card, **then** the card the link named when the page opened (the entry card) is placed first in the random deck, without duplicates and within the 1000-card deck limit, so the counter, Prev and Next include it and moving between cards never drops it. For a category link the card must belong to the URL's category; when the link carries `nav` or `keywords` filters, or the card is private, it is not added and the deck is the random set as before. The deck waits for the entry card to load before the first card is shown (#288).
- **AC 3.6.19** [Anyone] **Given** I play a Practice quiz (not Test, Learn, Daily, or a fixed set), **when** the current item's correct answer is *typeable* (at most 4 words and entirely Latin script once `{{lang|native|...}}` markup is replaced by its native text), **then** a per-item **Type answer** checkbox (off by default, not a scope-wide mode) appears above the options; non-typeable items stay multiple choice and show the note "This answer is too long to type, so choose an option." With it on, the options are replaced by a labelled text input **Type your answer** (autocomplete, autocorrect, autocapitalize and spellcheck off) submitted with Enter or **Check answer**. Grading is lenient: case, accents (NFKD), punctuation, apostrophes and a leading *a/an/the* are ignored; the correct answer is accepted in any alternate form split on `/`, the word `or` and parentheses (the text without the parenthetical, with it inlined, and the parenthetical alone; separators inside parentheses do not split); each word tolerates typos by the expected word's length (no typos under 4 letters or for words with digits, 1 edit for 4-7 letters, 2 for 8 or more) with the same word count required. The result is announced in a polite live region ("Correct." or "Incorrect." plus "Accepted answer: ...") and the usual answer panel shows. The answer is held, not yet scored, until I choose **Next question**/**Finish** (or Enter) or leave the item by any navigation; before then an incorrect typed answer shows **I was right**, which flips it to correct. When recorded it locks like a choice (first answer wins, one `question_answered` event with the final correctness, no new event fields, no typed text sent anywhere), the outcome stores `inputMode: "typed"` with the typed text as its response (multiple-choice outcomes store `inputMode: "choice"`), and a restored typed answer shows locked with no override. Letter and digit shortcuts are off while typing is selected. Test mode is unchanged (no Type answer, choice outcomes only) and Learn is unchanged. The grader (`features/items/quiz/typedAnswer.ts`) is exported for later reuse.
- **AC 3.6.20** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `GET /api/study-sessions/activity?days=N` (default 182, minimum 1, maximum 365), **then** I receive only my own activity: `today` (the server's UTC day), `days`, one `activity` entry per UTC day (`yyyy-MM-dd`) in the last N days that has at least one completed study session or a completed daily quiz, each with `sessions`, `questions`, `correct` and `hasDaily`, plus `currentStreak` and `longestStreak`. Days are grouped by the UTC date of `CompletedAt`; no per-session data is returned. Quiz and Learn sessions both count, and sessions imported from guest history (AC 3.6.16) count too. A streak is consecutive UTC days with at least one completed session; it is computed over all retained sessions (365 days) regardless of `days`, and the current streak is alive when the latest active day is today or yesterday (the same rule as the daily quiz streak, AC 3.13.4), otherwise 0. `hasDaily` is true on days I completed the daily quiz. Values of `days` outside 1 to 365 return 400, and anonymous callers receive 401.
- **AC 3.6.21** [Authenticated] **Given** I am signed in, **when** I open **My Progress** (`/progress`, linked from the main navigation and from the profile dialog), **then** I see streak cards (**Current streak**, **Longest streak**, in days), totals from `GET /api/study-sessions/summary` (sessions, questions, accuracy, last 30 days) and a 26-week activity calendar from `GET /api/study-sessions/activity?days=182`. The calendar is Sunday-first week columns of UTC days; each day has an accessible name such as "3 sessions on Oct 21" (", daily quiz completed" when it applies), five intensity levels (0, 1, 2, 3 to 4, 5 or more sessions) that are not colour-only (active days show their session count, a legend names the levels, and days with a completed daily quiz carry a marker), a focusable scroll region so it scrolls sideways inside its own container at 320px without widening the page, and a "Show activity as a list" text alternative. The page states that streaks use UTC days and count completed quiz and Learn sessions only (review-queue answers and flashcard study are not counted yet), and, when I have imported sessions, that imported runs are unverified but count toward the calendar and streaks. A failed load shows an alert with **Try again**; with no sessions the page offers **Play the daily quiz** and **Browse categories**.
- **AC 3.6.22** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not signed in, **when** I open `/progress` (the route is public; it does not redirect to sign in), **then** the page shows a guest view built only from the quiz history saved in this browser (AC 3.6.13): the same calendar and streaks, labelled **This browser only**, with no API call, plus the inline `track-progress` signup prompt (AC 4.2.10) that returns me to `/progress` after sign-up. Nothing in the guest view is described as saved to an account. The **My Progress** navigation link is shown only when signed in.
- **AC 3.6.23** [Authenticated] **Given** I open the **Profile** dialog (AC 4.5.4), **then** it shows a **Study progress** block with my total sessions, questions and accuracy from `GET /api/study-sessions/summary` (accuracy "None yet" when I have answered nothing) and a **View my progress** link to `/progress` that closes the dialog. If the totals cannot be loaded the block says so and the rest of the profile still works.

### AC 3.7 Keyword descriptions (breadcrumb)

**API**

- **AC 3.7.1** [Anyone] **Given** I call `GET /keywords/descriptions?category={name}&keywords=...`, **then** the API returns the description for each keyword in the path (for breadcrumb tooltips); category and keywords use the same resolution as elsewhere.

**UI**

- **AC 3.7.2** [Anyone] **Given** I am on a category page with keywords in the path, **when** the breadcrumb is shown, **then** I can see keyword descriptions (e.g. tooltips) per API where available.

---

### AC 3.8 Admin: categories and keywords (create, update, delete)

**API**

- **AC 3.8.1** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call create/update/delete endpoints for categories or keyword relations (e.g. CreateCategory, UpdateCategory, DeleteCategory, CreateCategoryKeyword/create relation, UpdateCategoryKeyword, DeleteCategoryKeyword), **then** the API allows the operation; categories remain public.
- **AC 3.8.2** [Authenticated (non-admin)] **Given** I am authenticated but not an admin, **when** I call create/update/delete for categories or category keywords, **then** the API returns 403 Forbidden.
- **AC 3.8.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call those endpoints, **then** the API returns 401.

**UI**

- **AC 3.8.4** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I use the Admin Categories / Keywords UI, **then** I can create, update, and delete categories and keyword relations (parent/child per category, sort order, description); the UI reflects API rules.

---

### AC 3.9 Filters on the Categories page

On the Categories page (Sets view and List view), a **filter dialog** lets the user change the category/keyword scope and apply **scope filters** (item type, search, rating). Filters are reflected in the URL; applying them navigates and updates the **scope**. The dialog is opened by a **Filters** button and closes via **Show results** or the close (×) button. On all screen sizes it appears as a centered modal (not a bottom sheet).

**Filter dialog (open/close and active state)**

- **AC 3.9.1** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the Categories page in Sets or List view, **when** the page loads, **then** I see a **Filters** button in the control bar; clicking it opens the filter dialog as a centered modal. The dialog closes when I click **Show results**, press Escape, or click the backdrop. When I click **Show results** after applying changes, the dialog closes.
- **AC 3.9.2** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the Categories page, **when** any filter is in effect (e.g. I am not on "All categories", or I have path/query keywords, or I have scope filters such as item type, search, or rating), **then** the Filters button shows an **Active** indicator (badge). Inside the dialog a **Clear all** link resets all filter controls to the default or current-scope state.

**Keyword tag filters (Sets and List view)**

- **AC 3.9.2a** [Anyone] **Given** the filter dialog is open in **List** view, **then** I see a **Keywords** section at the top with a search input. As I type, a dropdown suggests up to **5** matching keyword names from the currently loaded items (in-memory match, no network call). Selecting a suggestion adds that keyword to the active query filters immediately (the URL updates); the input resets so I can add more keywords. Active keyword filters are shown as removable chips beneath the input. Pressing **Enter** in the input field adds the typed text as a keyword filter when it exactly matches a suggestion; if no suggestions exist, pressing Enter adds the free-text value as a filter.
- **AC 3.9.2b** [Anyone] **Given** the filter dialog is open in **Sets** view for a specific category, **then** I see a **Keywords** section at the top with a search input. As I type, a dropdown suggests up to **5** matching item-level tag keyword names fetched from the API for that category (cached per category, no call for "All categories"). Selecting a suggestion, or pressing **Enter** to submit free text, adds that keyword to `?keywords=` in the URL immediately while keeping the Sets view (`?view=sets`); the input resets so I can add more. Active keyword filters are shown as removable chips beneath the input; clicking a chip removes that keyword from the URL.

**Category and keyword filters (navigation scope)**

- **AC 3.9.3** [Anyone] **Given** the filter dialog is visible, **when** I use the filters, **then** I see: (1) a **Category** dropdown (e.g. "All categories" or a specific category), (2) an optional **Primary topic** (rank-1 keyword) control, and (3) an optional **Subtopic** (rank-2 keyword) control, disabled until a primary topic is selected. I can click **Apply** to navigate to the selected category/keyword scope; the URL path and optional query keywords are updated, page is reset to 1, and the dialog closes.
- **AC 3.9.4** [Anyone] **Given** I have selected "All categories" in the filter dialog and optionally one or more keywords, **when** I click Apply, **then** the scope becomes all categories with those keywords as query filters (e.g. slug `all` or `/categories` with `?keywords=...`); the path does not include category/keyword segments.

**Scope filters (item type, search, rating)**

- **AC 3.9.5** [Anyone] **Given** the filter dialog is visible, **when** I add **scope filters**, **then** I can add one or more of: **item type** (e.g. all / public / private), **search** (text), and **rating** (min, max, include unrated, only unrated). Each added scope filter is shown with a remove control. When I click **Apply**, the selected scope filters are reflected in the URL (e.g. `filterType`, `search`, `ratingMin`, `ratingMax`, `ratingUnrated`, `ratingOnlyUnrated`) and applied to the scope. Item type is sent to the items API where supported; search and rating may be applied client-side to the current result set where applicable.
- **AC 3.9.6** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the Categories List view with scope filters applied (e.g. search or rating), **when** the list is loaded, **then** the items shown are narrowed by those filters; pagination applies to the filtered set. Changing or clearing scope filters and applying updates the list accordingly.
- **AC 3.9.6a** [Anyone] **Given** I am paging through list-mode items in Categories or Collection detail, **when** the current page renders, **then** I see paging controls above the list and a **Previous / Next** pager below the list so I can continue navigating without scrolling back to the top.

**URL and scope**

- **AC 3.9.7** [Anyone] **Given** I have applied filters on the Categories page, **when** the URL is updated, **then** the category/keyword scope is encoded in the path and (for query keywords) in the `keywords` query parameter; scope filter parameters (e.g. `filterType`, `search`, `ratingMin`, `ratingMax`, `ratingUnrated`, `ratingOnlyUnrated`) are encoded in the query. Refreshing or sharing the URL restores the same filter state. When I navigate to a different category/keyword path (e.g. via breadcrumb or bucket), scope filter query parameters may be cleared so the new path has a clean scope unless the UI preserves them.

### AC 3.10 Taxonomy (YAML), public API, and item ingress

**Design:** The canonical category tree and navigation keyword slugs are defined in `docs/quizymode_taxonomy.yaml`. At API startup the file is loaded into an in-memory taxonomy registry. Database categories and `KeywordRelation` navigation rows are seeded from that same YAML (plus per-category **other** where applicable). Item create, update, bulk add, collection upload, and study-guide finalize must not introduce new public categories or ad-hoc public navigation paths.

**API**

- **AC 3.10.1** [Anyone] **Given** the API is running, **when** I call `GET /taxonomy` (no auth required), **then** the response lists all taxonomy categories with nested L1 → L2 navigation slugs (and optional flat slug lists) sourced only from the in-memory registry, not from ad-hoc DB growth.
- **AC 3.10.1a** [Anyone] **Given** I browse category navigation in the SPA, **when** taxonomy-backed pages load, **then** they use `GET /taxonomy` as the canonical tree of category to primary-topic (L1) to subtopic (L2) paths for valid `/categories/...` navigation scopes.
- **AC 3.10.2** [Authenticated] **Given** I create or update an item (single `POST /items`, `PUT /items/{id}`, `POST /items/bulk`, upload-to-collection, or study-guide finalize), **when** I supply a category name, **then** it must match a taxonomy category **and** exist already as a **public** row in `Categories`; the API does **not** create new global categories as a side effect of item ingress.
- **AC 3.10.2a** [Admin] **Given** I call the repo-managed seed-sync endpoints (`POST /admin/seed-sync/preview` or `POST /admin/seed-sync/apply`), **when** an item payload supplies a category name, **then** it must match a taxonomy category **and** an existing public `Categories` row; seed sync does not create new public categories.
- **AC 3.10.3** [Authenticated] **Given** I supply `navigationKeyword1` and `navigationKeyword2` on item ingress, **when** the pair is not a valid (L1, L2) path for that category in the taxonomy, **then** the request fails validation; bulk, import, and admin seed-sync flows do **not** auto-create missing `KeywordRelation` rows for navigation.
- **AC 3.10.4** [Authenticated] **Given** I attach extra keywords beyond the two navigation slugs in the regular user-facing item ingress flows (`POST /items`, `PUT /items/{id}`, `POST /items/bulk`, upload-to-collection, or study-guide finalize), **when** a keyword slug matches a taxonomy slug for that category, **then** it is attached as a **public** keyword without review pending; **when** it does not match taxonomy, **then** it is stored as a **private** user keyword with **review pending** (including for admins using those same non-seed-sync flows). Extras are matched on **exact** slug equality only (edit-distance similarity is not used).
- **AC 3.10.5** [Anyone] **Given** Discover item filters use `category` and `keywords` (L1/L2), **when** the keyword path is invalid for that category under the same taxonomy rules as items, **then** the API returns **400** (see also AC 1.9.1b).

**UI**

- **AC 3.10.6** [Authenticated] **Given** I use Create Item, Edit Item, Bulk Create, or upload flows that pick category and navigation, **when** the form loads, **then** the **Category** list is driven from `GET /taxonomy` (fixed taxonomy categories), and **Primary topic** / **Subtopic** are required dropdowns populated from that taxonomy for the selected category; I cannot type arbitrary navigation slugs for L1/L2. **Additional keywords** may suggest taxonomy slugs; free-text extras are treated as private pending on the server per AC 3.10.4.

---

### AC 3.11 Languages taxonomy and language learning item conventions

**Context:** The `languages` category is structured to support language learning experiences across multiple world languages. The taxonomy uses a two-tier keyword hierarchy; item-level keywords (additional keywords beyond the two navigation slugs) carry frequency and situational metadata.

**Taxonomy structure:**

- Each promoted language (English, Spanish, French, German, Italian, Russian, Japanese, Latvian, Ukrainian, Bulgarian, Latin, Romanian, Portuguese, Arabic, Chinese, Korean) has its own **rank-1 keyword** under the `languages` category.
- The `languages` category exposes only **language-name rank-1 keywords** plus one catch-all rank-1 bucket: `other-langs`.
- Each promoted language keeps language-specific rank-2 subtopics such as `core`, `phrases`, `vocab`, `grammar`, `idioms`, `conversation`, and `travel` where applicable. Some languages have additional subtopics (for example `hiragana`, `katakana`, `kanji` for Japanese; `expressions` for Latin; `reading`, `writing`, `pronunciation`, and `listening` for English; `characters` for Chinese; `hangul` for Korean).
- Less-developed or uncategorized language content stays under the `other-langs` rank-1 keyword and can be promoted later as content grows.
- Legacy non-language rank-1 buckets under `languages` (`esl`, `travel`, `vocab`, `grammar`, `idioms`, `general`, `mixed`) are not valid browse roots anymore. Existing rows using those paths must be migrated to a language-specific path (usually `english/...`) or to `other-langs/mixed`.

**Rank-2 subtopic definitions:**

| Subtopic | Scope |
|---|---|
| `core` | Essential beginner content: numbers 1–20, greetings, yes/no, please/thank you, introductions, water/bread |
| `phrases` | Situational phrases: café/restaurant orders, directions, prices/shopping, hotel check-in, emergency |
| `vocab` | Thematic vocabulary lists |
| `grammar` | Grammar rules and patterns |
| `idioms` | Common idioms and expressions |
| `conversation` | Conversational practice dialogs and patterns |
| `expressions` (Latin only) | Famous Latin expressions with English translation and context |

**Item-level keyword conventions for language learning items:**

Items in the `languages` category should use item-level keywords (the `keywords` array in seed data / additional keywords in the UI) to carry:

- **Frequency tiers:** `freq-100` (top ~100 essential items per language), `freq-500`, `freq-1000` — used to filter curated vocabulary sets without constraining navigation paths to exact counts.
- **Situational tags:** `situation:cafe`, `situation:directions`, `situation:prices`, `situation:greetings`, `situation:intro`, `situation:hotel`, `situation:emergency` — allow cross-language situational filtering.
- **CEFR levels:** `cefr:a1`, `cefr:a2`, `cefr:b1`, `cefr:b2`, `cefr:c1`, `cefr:c2` — indicate difficulty level.

These item-level keywords are **not** navigation keywords. They are stored as public taxonomy-matched keywords when they match the taxonomy slug list, or as private pending keywords otherwise. They are available as item tag filters in Discover and the Categories keyword filter.

**ACs:**

- **AC 3.11.1** [Anyone] **Given** a user browses `/categories/languages/{language}/core`, **when** the scope loads, **then** it shows items whose `navigationKeyword1` = that language and `navigationKeyword2` = `core`; items tagged `freq-100` are retrievable by adding `?keywords=freq-100` as an item tag filter.
- **AC 3.11.2** [Anyone] **Given** a user browses `/categories/languages/latin/expressions`, **when** the scope loads, **then** it shows items in the famous Latin expressions set; each item's question is the Latin phrase and the correct answer is the English translation with context.
- **AC 3.11.3** [Anyone] **Given** `other-langs` rank-1 still exists as a catch-all, **when** items reference `navigationKeyword1 = "other-langs"`, **then** those items remain accessible at `/categories/languages/other-langs/mixed`; Arabic, Chinese, Korean, and Portuguese are now promoted to their own rank-1 groups and their `core` subtopic is accessible at `/categories/languages/{language}/core`.
- **AC 3.11.4** [Admin/System] **Given** the API starts or an admin seed-sync apply runs, **when** legacy `languages` items still use retired rank-1 paths such as `esl`, `travel`, `vocab`, `grammar`, `idioms`, `general`, or `mixed`, **then** the database normalizes those items to the current language-first taxonomy before continuing so browse counts and navigation remain aligned with `GET /taxonomy`.

---

### AC 3.12 Item pronunciation and language-aware speech

**Context:** Items can carry optional per-field speech metadata (`pronunciation`, `languageCode`) to support native-script content and browser text-to-speech (TTS). This is designed for items whose question or answer _is_ a foreign-language word or phrase, not for items that already describe that word in English.

**ACs:**

- **AC 3.12.1** [Anyone] **Given** an item has speech metadata on question, correct answer, or an incorrect answer — each field carrying an optional `pronunciation` string and an optional BCP-47 `languageCode` — **when** the browser supports the Web Speech API, **then** the Speak button reads the field's text using a voice matching `languageCode`; if no matching voice is available and `pronunciation` is set, the `pronunciation` string is spoken instead; if neither applies, standard English TTS is used.
- **AC 3.12.2** [Anyone] **Given** an item field has a `pronunciation` value that differs from the displayed text (e.g. romaji under a kanji question), **when** the field is rendered in Explore card view, Quiz option, or Item Detail, **then** a muted hint line showing the pronunciation is displayed directly beneath the field text.
- **AC 3.12.3** [Authenticated] **Given** I use Create Item or Edit Item, **when** the form loads, **then** optional pronunciation and language-code inputs are available for question, correct answer, and each incorrect answer; leaving them blank persists no speech metadata.
- **AC 3.12.4** [Admin/Seed] **Given** a repo-managed seed JSON file includes `questionSpeech`, `correctAnswerSpeech`, or `incorrectAnswerSpeech` fields, **when** seed-sync apply runs, **then** those fields are normalised (trimmed, truncated to limits) and persisted; a seed file that omits these fields does not clear existing speech metadata on a re-sync.
- **AC 3.12.5** [Design intent] Speech metadata should be set only when the question or answer **is** a native-script word or phrase (e.g. `"黒い"`). Items whose question is already a full English sentence wrapping a foreign word (e.g. `"What does '黒い' mean?"`) should omit `languageCode` — the TTS already reads the sentence correctly as English — and may omit `pronunciation` too.
- **AC 3.12.6** [Anyone] **Given** a text field (question, answer, explanation) contains inline foreign-phrase markup in the format `{{lang|native|translit|pronunciation}}`, **when** the text is rendered in any item view (Explore, Quiz, Item List, Item Detail), **then**: (a) the native script is displayed inline where the markup appears; (b) if `translit` or `pronunciation` is non-empty, a muted hint line is rendered beneath showing `translit · pronunciation` (or whichever parts are present); (c) the surrounding English text is shown as-is; (d) the Speak button reads each segment in its correct language — English segments with the default voice and foreign segments with the voice matching `lang` (BCP-47); (e) text without any markup is rendered and spoken identically to before this feature. The format is: `lang` = BCP-47 tag (e.g. `ja-JP`, `ru-RU`), `native` = text displayed and spoken in target language, `translit` = romanization (optional, shown in hint), `pronunciation` = English-speaker-friendly guide with stressed syllable in CAPS (optional, shown in hint after `·`).
- **AC 3.12.7** [Authenticated] **Given** I use Create/Edit Item and leave an incorrect-answer field blank (empty or whitespace), **when** I submit, **then** the blank field is dropped and the remaining incorrect answers' pronunciation/language are sent under each answer's new position (e.g. A, blank, C with speech on C sends `incorrectAnswers` `["A","C"]` and `incorrectAnswerSpeech` `{1: ...}`); speech never attaches to another answer and the request is never rejected for an out-of-range key; speech entered under a blank answer is discarded (#269).
