## 4. Users and auth

Authentication uses **email + password** with secure password hashing and short-lived access tokens, or **Continue with Google** (AUTH-003, #118) where that is enabled for the deployment (`VITE_GOOGLE_SIGNIN_ENABLED`; off unless the Cognito/Google setup in `docs/infra/COGNITO_SETUP.md` is complete). Authorization is role-based (`User`, `Admin`) and relies on the global invariants in **Access and ownership**. This section describes **how users sign up, sign in, manage sessions, and view/update their profile**.

### AC 4.1 Authentication model and roles

**API**

- **AC 4.1.1** [Anyone] **Given** I call any **authenticated** endpoint without a valid access token, **when** the endpoint requires a logged-in user (e.g. creating items, collections, or reading my settings), **then** the API returns **401 Unauthorized** with an `application/problem+json` ProblemDetails body and exactly one `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` header (no `error` parameter when no token was sent); the body and header never contain token-validation details (no `error_description`/`error_uri`). No partial data is leaked. Handler-level 401s (a valid token whose UserId cannot be resolved, AC 4.1.5) also return an `application/problem+json` ProblemDetails body (title `Unauthorized`); they carry no `invalid_token` challenge because the token itself was valid.
- **AC 4.1.2** [Authenticated / Admin] **Given** I am authenticated and call an endpoint I do not have permission to use (e.g. non-admin calling an admin-only endpoint), **when** the resource exists and its existence is not secret, **then** the API returns **403 Forbidden** with an `application/problem+json` ProblemDetails body (title `Forbidden`, no policy name, no `WWW-Authenticate`), whether from an authorization policy or `Results.Forbid()`; for resources whose existence must be hidden (e.g. other users' private collections or items), the API returns **404 Not Found** per global invariants. Collection writes follow this rule (Invariant C5, #289): a non-owner gets 404 on a private collection and 403 on a public one, whose ProblemDetails body adds a rule-specific `detail` (like items, #286).
- **AC 4.1.3** [Anyone] **Given** I have an access token (e.g. JWT) issued by the system, **when** I include it in the `Authorization: Bearer {token}` header on API calls, **then** the backend validates issuer, audience, signature and lifetime (5-minute clock skew); expired or invalid tokens are treated as missing and result in **401 Unauthorized** with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token"` (no `error_description`). The API does **not** check revocation: a revoked token, or one belonging to a disabled or deleted user, is accepted until it expires.
- **AC 4.1.4** [Anyone] **Given** the backend needs user identity, **when** a request is authenticated, **then** the token contains at least `sub` (user id) and `email`. There is no `role` claim: Admin access is decided solely by the token's `cognito:groups` claim, with no server-side check. A caller is an admin when any group value starts with `admin` (case-insensitive; e.g. `Admins`). The same rule is used by the `Admin` authorization policy, the API's `IUserContext.IsAdmin` and the SPA (which reads the access token, then the ID token); the API evaluates the bearer (ID) token.
- **AC 4.1.5** [System] **Given** an authenticated request carries a `sub` that has no `Users` row yet and another row already has the same email (case-insensitively), **when** `UserUpsertMiddleware` runs, **then** it re-points that row to the new `sub` only if the token's `email_verified` claim is `true` (JSON boolean or string, any case); a row that already belongs to this `sub` is always used as-is. If the claim is `false` or absent, the existing row is left unchanged (no `Subject` change, no second row, no email update), a warning naming only the `sub` is logged, `UserId` is not resolved for the request, and the request continues. The same rule applies when a concurrent first sign-in loses the unique-email race.
- **AC 4.1.6** [System] **Given** a token was issued by Cognito for a Google sign-in, **when** the API validates it, **then** issuer, audience, signature and lifetime are validated exactly as for password sign-ins and `cognito:groups` controls Admin access regardless of identity provider; the API has no provider-specific branches.
- **AC 4.1.7** [Anyone] **Given** any rate-limited endpoint rejects a request, **then** the API returns **429 Too Many Requests** with an `application/problem+json` ProblemDetails body (`title` "Too Many Requests", `status` 429, `type` `https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6585#section-4`, and an endpoint-specific `detail`), plus a `Retry-After` header (seconds) when the limiter reports one; the body carries no partition key, user id or IP address.

### AC 4.2 Registration (sign up)

There is no first-party `/auth/register` endpoint. Sign-up, confirmation, and credential storage are handled directly by Amplify against the configured Cognito user pool; `usersApi.checkAvailability` is the only first-party pre-check (duplicate display name/email), and `POST /users/policy-acceptances` is the only first-party write this flow makes (see AC 4.6.5).

**UI**

- **AC 4.2.1** [Anonymous] **Given** I open the **Sign up** page with no `reason` (or an unknown one), **when** the page renders, **then** I see the default benefit-led heading **"Make your next study session yours."** and a one-sentence description of a benefit that exists in the deployed release; an allowlisted `reason` (`save-progress`, `keep-collection`, `create-content`, `join-discussion`, `review-later`, `track-progress`) swaps in reason-specific copy from a fixed lookup, never echoing the raw query value. Before **PROGRESS-001** (#114) and **CLAIM-003** (#115) ship, `save-progress` copy uses the heading **"Make your next quiz yours"**, states that quiz scores are not saved to accounts yet, and offers only existing benefits (bookmarking public collections, building private practice sets); it never claims the result is saved, kept, synced or imported, in the browser or in an account (a signed-in return does not restore the guest run, AC 3.6.12).
- **AC 4.2.2** [Anonymous] **Given** I open the **Sign up** page, **then** the display-name, email, and password fields have persistent visible labels (not placeholder-only), the password field is a single field with a keyboard-accessible show/hide toggle, and a hint states the minimum length before I submit. Terms of Service / Privacy Policy links and the required acknowledgement checkbox remain present and must be checked to submit.
- **AC 4.2.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I submit the sign-up form, **when** the display name equals the email, the password is under 8 characters, the legal acknowledgement is unchecked, or `usersApi.checkAvailability` reports the display name or email already taken, **then** submission is blocked with an inline error and no Cognito `signUp` call is made.
- **AC 4.2.4** [Anonymous] **Given** sign-up succeeds, **when** the UI transitions to the confirmation step, **then** it shows the destination email, starts a 60-second resend cooldown, and persists only the email and cooldown timing (never a password or code) to tab-scoped storage that expires after 24 hours, so a reload during confirmation can resume without losing the pending email.
- **AC 4.2.5** [Anonymous] **Given** I am on the confirmation step, **when** I submit a code, **then** the UI calls Cognito's `confirmSignUp`, attempts auto-sign-in when the provider reports an auto-sign-in step, and verifies a real session before navigating. A mismatched or expired code keeps the email and intent and explains how to retry or resend; a "use a different email" action clears the pending state and restarts the form.
- **AC 4.2.6** [Anonymous] **Given** confirmation completes, **when** a verified authenticated session was established (auto-sign-in succeeded), **then** the UI navigates directly to the validated `returnTo` destination from AC 4.3.5a without requiring a manual sign-in; **when** no real session can be verified (auto-sign-in unavailable, expired, or interrupted), **then** the UI shows **"Account confirmed — sign in to continue"** with a link to **Sign in** that preserves the destination, and does not ask me to register again.
- **AC 4.2.7** [Anonymous] **Given** I am on the confirmation step, **when** the 60-second cooldown has elapsed, **then** I can resend the code; resend failures (including provider throttling) show an actionable message and do not restart the cooldown on failure.
- **AC 4.2.8** [Anonymous] **Given** a sign-up or confirmation-code error is shown, **then** it appears in an alert region (announced by assistive technology), the offending field(s) are marked `aria-invalid` and linked to the message with `aria-describedby`, and focus moves to the first such field (display name and email when they match; password; the legal acknowledgement checkbox; whichever of display name or email the availability check rejected; the confirmation code). Errors with no single field (provider failures, resend failures) are announced without moving focus. The state clears on the next submit or resend. Every text input shows a visible focus ring (not only a border-colour change), and a long email address wraps on the confirmation step so the page never scrolls horizontally at 195×422, 390×844 or 1280×900.
- **AC 4.2.9** [Anonymous] **Given** I accepted the Terms of Service and Privacy Policy on the sign-up form, **when** I later have a verified session (after confirming the account, or on any later sign-in or refresh), **then** the queued acceptance is matched to my signup email whether Cognito reports that email on the access token, as the username, or only on the ID token (an access token whose username is the account's sub must not hide it), is recorded once via `POST /users/policy-acceptances`, and is removed from the browser only after the API accepts it; if the call fails the queue is kept and retried on the next refresh.
- **AC 4.2.10** [Anonymous] **Given** a contextual signup prompt (CONV-001, #109) is placed next to an action, **then** it is an inline, non-blocking panel (never a modal, interstitial, or page-load/timer/scroll/exit-intent prompt) whose heading, body and primary action come from a fixed, truthful lookup for one allowlisted `reason` (`save-progress`, `keep-collection`, `create-content`, `join-discussion`, `review-later`, `track-progress`); before **PROGRESS-001** (#114) and **CLAIM-003** (#115) ship, no copy claims a quiz score is saved to, synced with, or imported into an account. The primary action links to **Sign up** with that `reason` and the current page (path, query, hash) as `returnTo`, validated by AC 4.3.5a; a secondary **Sign in** link carries the same intent. The prompt is not rendered while authentication is resolving or when I am signed in. **Not now** dismisses it in place without changing the page underneath, and a dismissed reason stays hidden for the rest of the tab session (tab-scoped storage, surviving reloads and new runs in that tab; a new tab or browser session may show it again). Prompts for other reasons are unaffected.
- **AC 4.2.11** [Anonymous] **Given** I finish a quiz run (any scope, including the home page quiz) and see the **Quiz complete** summary (AC 3.6.9), **then** the `save-progress` prompt (AC 4.2.10) appears below the summary actions with the heading **"Make your next quiz yours"**, copy stating that quiz scores are not saved to accounts yet and that an account lets me bookmark public collections and build private practice sets, and a **Create a free account** action. It never appears during the run (including on the first question) or on **Review mistakes**, and dismissing it keeps the score, **Review mistakes**, **Play again** and browse actions as they were. Signed-in users never see it. The `review-later` reason is placed on the Flashcards end-of-deck summary (**AC 3.14.9**); the `track-progress` reason ("Track your progress across visits") is placed on the anonymous **My Progress** page (**AC 3.6.22**); the other reasons are supported by the component but not yet placed.

### AC 4.3 Login and tokens

**API**

- **AC 4.3.0** [System] **Given** an authenticated user makes any API request, **when** the middleware processes the JWT, **then** a `LoginSuccess` audit entry is written at most once per 10-minute window per user; subsequent requests within that window (token refreshes, page navigations) do not generate additional audit entries.
- **AC 4.3.0a** [System] **Given** the admin views audit logs, the user activity list, or the page-view analytics recent-views, **when** an IP address is present, **then** the API resolves it to a 2-letter ISO country code via ipinfo.io (free tier, HTTPS); results are cached in-process for the application lifetime; loopback and RFC-1918 private addresses resolve to "Local"; failures return null and do not block the response.
- **AC 4.3.0b** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/audit-logs`, **when** the `AuditLogs:ExcludedEmails` config list is non-empty, **then** audit entries belonging to those users are silently excluded from all results (anonymous entries with no `UserId` are not excluded); the exclusion applies before pagination so counts are accurate.
- **AC 4.3.0c** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/audit-logs?userEmail={email}`, **when** the email matches a known user, **then** only audit entries for that user are returned; **when** the email is unknown, an empty result set is returned; the user-email filter is applied after the configured exclusion list.

- **AC 4.3.1** [Anonymous] **Given** I submit **Sign in**, **then** the SPA signs in directly against the Cognito user pool with Amplify `signIn` (SRP; the app client allows `ALLOW_USER_SRP_AUTH` and `ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH`, see `docs/infra/COGNITO_SETUP.md`), so my password never reaches the Quizymode API and the API has no first-party `/auth/login` or `/auth/refresh` endpoint. Once sign-in completes the app reads the session with `fetchAuthSession` and stores the ID and access tokens in browser storage (navigation per AC 4.3.5).
- **AC 4.3.2** [Authenticated] **Given** I make an API request, **then** the SPA sends the stored Cognito ID token as `Authorization: Bearer` (falling back to the access token only when no ID token is stored). The API only validates it: issuer, audience (the app client id unless `Authentication:Cognito:Audience` is configured), signature against the pool's keys, and lifetime with a 5-minute clock skew (AC 4.1.3, 4.1.6). It never issues, refreshes or revokes tokens; `POST /auth/logout` only writes a `Logout` audit entry.
- **AC 4.3.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I enter an unknown email or a wrong password, or Cognito throttles repeated attempts, **then** Cognito rejects the Amplify `signIn` call and the Quizymode API is not involved: it returns no 401 or 429 for credential checks and has no sign-in rate limit of its own. The API has no endpoint for recording failed sign-ins (`POST /auth/login-failed` was removed in 3.84.0, #235; the path now returns 404). The `LoginFailed` audit action remains in the audit-log filter for historical rows, but nothing writes it.
- **AC 4.3.4** [Authenticated] **Given** my stored token expires within 60 seconds, **when** the SPA is about to call the API, **then** it calls `fetchAuthSession({ forceRefresh: true })` (Cognito refresh token) and stores the new tokens before the request; if that refresh fails it clears the stored tokens and sends the request without `Authorization` (no redirect). **Given** the API returns 401, **then** the SPA refreshes once and retries once; only if the refresh fails, or the retried request returns 401 again, it clears the tokens and sends the browser to `/`, except on public routes and for failed URLs matching public endpoints (`/categories`, `/keywords`, `/items`, `/collections`); a 500 on the retry changes nothing. Separately, `AuthContext.refreshAuth` runs on app load, and on window focus or when the tab becomes visible while I am signed in or a token is stored: it calls `fetchAuthSession()`, forces a refresh if the access or ID token has expired, and clears auth state if that fails (AC 4.3.7). Refresh-token lifetime and rotation are Cognito app-client settings, not API behavior. **Given** the API returns 403 (a valid token without permission, e.g. a non-admin on an admin endpoint, editing another user's comment, another user's public item (AC 2.1.12) or another user's public collection (Invariant C5)), **then** the SPA does not refresh, retry, clear tokens or redirect; the error goes to the calling page and I stay signed in (AC 4.1.2). A 403 on the retry after a 401 refresh is likewise passed on without clearing tokens. A change to my Cognito groups therefore takes effect at the next token refresh or sign-in.

**UI**

- **AC 4.3.5** [Anonymous] **Given** I open the **Sign in** page, **when** I submit valid credentials, **then** the UI navigates only once the provider reports completed sign-in and the app has verified an authenticated session (not merely a resolved promise), storing tokens and updating app state to "authenticated" before redirecting me to the validated `returnTo` destination carried in the URL, or home when none is present.
- **AC 4.3.5a** [Anonymous] **Given** an anonymous visitor is sent to a protected route, **when** `RequireAuthRoute` redirects them, **then** it redirects to **Sign in** carrying the current path/query/hash as a validated `returnTo` (and an allowlisted `reason` when known); after successful sign-in, signup, or confirmation, the app returns to that exact destination with its query intact, applying normal authorization there (a non-admin returning to an admin URL still gets the existing denied/fallback behavior, not another login loop).
- **AC 4.3.5b** [Anonymous] **Given** a `returnTo` value is present anywhere in the auth flow, **when** it is used to build a redirect, **then** it is validated against this app's own origin (same-origin, leading single `/`, no backslashes/control characters, bounded length, not a login/signup destination); an invalid, unsafe, missing, or duplicated value falls back to home rather than being used as-is. Already-authenticated visitors who land on Sign in/Sign up also redirect to the validated destination once auth loading settles.
- **AC 4.3.6** [Anonymous] **Given** I submit credentials and Amplify `signIn` fails or does not complete, **then** the UI shows fixed copy in an alert region, never Cognito's raw message (the Quizymode API is not involved); no token is stored and I am not navigated. The copy is chosen by Amplify error name or sign-in step, not by message text:

  | Cause | Copy |
  |---|---|
  | `NotAuthorizedException`, `UserNotFoundException` (identical, so an unknown email is indistinguishable from a wrong password) | "Incorrect email or password." |
  | `LimitExceededException`, `TooManyRequestsException`, `TooManyFailedAttemptsException` | "Too many sign-in attempts. Wait a few minutes and try again." |
  | Sign-in step `CONFIRM_SIGN_UP` | "This account hasn't been confirmed yet. Please contact us through the Feedback page and we'll help you finish setting it up." |
  | Sign-in step `RESET_PASSWORD` | "This account needs a password reset before you can sign in. Please contact us through the Feedback page." |
  | Any other sign-in step | "This account needs a sign-in step this app doesn't support yet. Please contact us through the Feedback page." |
  | `NetworkError` | "We couldn't reach the sign-in service. Check your connection and try again." |
  | Anything else, or no session could be established | "Sign-in failed. Please try again." |

  "Feedback page" is a link to `/feedback`. Cognito reports an account lockout (too many failed passwords) as `NotAuthorizedException`, so a locked-out account also shows "Incorrect email or password." (this avoids revealing which accounts exist). There is no forgot-password or resend-confirmation flow in the SPA.
- **AC 4.3.7** [Authenticated] **Given** I am on an authenticated-only SPA route when my session expires or can no longer be refreshed, **when** the app re-checks auth state, **then** it clears local auth state; the next render of that protected route redirects to **Sign in** with the current destination preserved as `returnTo` (the same path as AC 4.3.5a), rather than silently bouncing to home. Separately, when an API call's 401 cannot be recovered (AC 4.3.4), the API client clears tokens and hard-navigates to `/`. On reload, if Amplify still has a valid session I am shown as signed in again; otherwise I am signed out. The original destination is not kept.
- **AC 4.3.8** [Anonymous] **Given** I open **Sign in** or **Sign up**, **when** `VITE_GOOGLE_SIGNIN_ENABLED` is exactly `true` and `VITE_COGNITO_DOMAIN` is set, **then** an "or" divider and a full-width **Continue with Google** button (official four-colour G, minimum 44px tall) appear below the primary button (Sign up also shows "By continuing with Google you agree to the Terms of Service and acknowledge the Privacy Policy" with links); otherwise (flag absent or not `true`, or domain missing, which also logs a console error) neither is rendered, the app loads normally and the password flows are unchanged. The button is not gated by the Sign up legal checkbox (acceptance happens in AC 4.6.10).
- **AC 4.3.9** [Anonymous] **Given** Google sign-in is enabled and I click **Continue with Google**, **then** the button shows **Redirecting to Google…** (disabled, `aria-busy`, announced by a status region) and the password submit is disabled, and the browser is sent to the Cognito hosted domain with the authorization-code + PKCE flow (public client, no client secret), `identity_provider=Google`, scopes `openid email profile`, redirect URI `/auth/callback`, and the validated `returnTo`/`reason` as custom state (also kept in tab storage and re-validated on read). If the redirect cannot start, the button resets, focus returns to it and the page shows "We couldn't start Google sign-in. Please try again, or use your email and password."; restoring the page from the back-forward cache also resets the button.
- **AC 4.3.10** [Anonymous] **Given** I return to `/auth/callback` from Google, **when** Amplify finishes the code exchange, **then** the page shows "Signing you in…" (focused, announced politely; after 10 seconds it adds "Still working…" and a **Back to sign in** link), verifies a real session, records `sign_in_completed` (AC 5.4.10) and replace-navigates to the validated `returnTo` (AC 4.3.5b); a tampered or external `returnTo` goes home. The outcome is captured at module scope before React renders, so a result that fires early is not lost; with no outcome and no code in flight the page uses an existing session or shows the generic error instead of spinning forever. Only `/auth/callback` consumes the outcome.
- **AC 4.3.11** [Anonymous] **Given** a CONFIRMED password account whose email is verified exists and I sign in with Google using the same verified email for the first time, **when** the Cognito Pre sign-up Lambda (`infra/cognito-presignup`) runs, **then** it links the Google identity to that account (`AdminLinkProviderForUser`) and aborts that attempt with `QM_ACCOUNT_LINKED`; the SPA retries the redirect exactly once (marker set before redirecting; the retry is silent and acts only on the Amplify failure event, matching the sentinel as a whole token) and I land in the same account: same Cognito `sub`, same `Users` row, same collections and admin group, and password sign-in still works. There is still one `Users` row per email. A same-email password account that is UNCONFIRMED is deleted by the Lambda (it never proved ownership) before the Google user is created; any other state (`FORCE_CHANGE_PASSWORD`, `RESET_REQUIRED`, confirmed but unverified) fails closed with a generic error.
- **AC 4.3.12** [Anonymous] **Given** Google reports `email_verified` as anything other than `true` (or omits it), **when** the Pre sign-up Lambda runs, **then** nothing is created or linked and the callback page shows "Verify your email with Google first, then try again." (a verified email with no confirmed password account simply creates a normal new account, AC 4.3.11).
- **AC 4.3.13** [Anonymous] **Given** the Google flow fails or is cancelled, **when** I land on `/auth/callback`, **then** the heading is "We couldn't sign you in" (or "Sign-in cancelled" for cancellation) and a fixed message from a lookup is shown in an alert (cancelled: "You cancelled sign-in with Google. No changes were made."; generic: "Something went wrong while signing you in with Google. Please try again."; a second `QM_ACCOUNT_LINKED`: "We couldn't finish signing you in with Google. Please try again, or use your email and password."); the raw Cognito `error_description` is never rendered, and **Back to sign in** / **Use a different email to sign up** keep the validated `returnTo`/`reason`. There is no further automatic retry.
- **AC 4.3.14** [Anonymous] **Given** I open Sign in or Sign up in a known embedded in-app browser (Instagram, Facebook, TikTok, Line, LinkedIn, Snapchat, Android WebView), where Google blocks sign-in, **then** the button is replaced by "Open this page in your browser to continue with Google." with a **Copy link** action (success "Link copied" for 3 seconds; failure "Couldn't copy the link. Select the address in your browser's address bar instead."); email and password still work.
- **AC 4.3.15** [Anonymous] **Given** a Google-only account uses an email, **when** someone submits password **Sign up** with that email, **then** the Pre sign-up Lambda rejects it with `QM_USE_GOOGLE`, no account is created and the Sign up page shows "This email uses Continue with Google" on the email field (the sentinel is never shown), whether or not the Google button is enabled.
- **AC 4.3.16** [System] **Given** the Cognito redirect carries `code`, `state`, `error` or `error_description`, **then** those parameters are never sent to page-view analytics (AC 5.3: stripped from every recorded query string, and `/auth/callback` itself is never recorded) or to front-end error reports (AC 5.6: report paths carry no query string), and first-visit attribution capture is skipped on `/auth/callback`.

### AC 4.4 Logout and token revocation

**API**

- **AC 4.4.1** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `POST /auth/logout`, **then** the API records a logout audit event for the current user and returns success (currently **200 OK** with a simple body). Authentication itself is managed by the external identity provider; this endpoint does not directly rotate passwords or manage provider refresh-token state.

**UI**

- **AC 4.4.2** [Authenticated] **Given** I am signed in and click **Sign out**, **when** the logout action is triggered, **then** the UI calls `POST /auth/logout` (best-effort), clears any locally stored access token or auth state, navigates me to a public page (e.g. home or login), and ensures that authenticated-only UI (e.g. Create, My collections) is no longer visible.
- **AC 4.4.3** [Authenticated] **Given** I signed in with Google and click **Sign out**, **when** logout runs, **then** the UI calls `POST /auth/logout`, clears the stored tokens, and Amplify redirects through the Cognito hosted `/logout` back to the site home (`/`); I am not signed out of my Google account. Password sign-out is unchanged.

### AC 4.5 Profile (view and update my info)

**API**

- **AC 4.5.1** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `GET /users/me`, **then** the API returns **200 OK** with my current app profile fields: `id`, `name`, `email`, `isAdmin`, `createdAt`, and `lastLogin`; it does **not** return password hashes, provider tokens, or other secrets.
- **AC 4.5.2** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `PUT /users/me` with `{ "name": "..." }`, **then** the API updates my display name / username and returns the updated profile payload. Attempts to change fields outside the supported request contract are not part of this endpoint.
- **AC 4.5.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `GET /users/me` or `PUT /users/me`, **then** the API returns **401 Unauthorized**.

**UI**

- **AC 4.5.4** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I open the **Profile** UI from the site chrome, **then** the UI shows my current profile in a modal, including non-editable fields (email, role/admin flag, created date, last login) and editable name; saving changes calls `PUT /users/me` and displays success or validation errors. The modal also shows my study totals and a link to **My Progress** (**AC 3.6.23**).

### AC 4.6 Password change and reset

Quizymode currently relies on the external identity provider for password, recovery, and confirmation flows. These behaviors are intentionally outside the app API documented here.

**Current app scope**

- **AC 4.6.1** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not signed in, **when** I visit the app, **then** I can access the implemented authentication screens for **Sign in** and **Sign up**.
- **AC 4.6.2** [User] **Given** I sign in, sign up, confirm signup, or recover account access, **when** those flows require password or recovery handling, **then** the behavior is provided by the configured identity provider rather than by first-party Quizymode API endpoints.
- **AC 4.6.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I am on the **Sign up** page, **when** I review or submit the form, **then** I can open the current **Terms of Service** and **Privacy Policy**, and the form does not continue unless I affirm them. **Continue with Google** (AC 4.3.8) is not gated by the checkbox; its acceptance is recorded through AC 4.6.10.
- **AC 4.6.4** [Anyone] **Given** I am anywhere in the app, **when** I use the global footer, **then** I can open the **About**, **Feedback**, **Privacy Policy**, and **Terms of Service** pages.
- **AC 4.6.5** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `POST /users/policy-acceptances` with one or more `{ policyType, policyVersion, acceptedAtUtc }` entries for the supported legal documents, **when** the request is valid, **then** the API records auditable acceptance rows for the current user with the submitted acceptance time plus the server-side recorded time; repeated submissions for the same `(user, policyType, policyVersion)` do not create duplicates.
- **AC 4.6.6** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `POST /users/policy-acceptances`, **then** the API returns **401 Unauthorized**.
- **AC 4.6.7** [Anonymous -> Authenticated] **Given** I successfully submit sign-up with the legal acknowledgement checked, **when** I later complete an authenticated session on the same browser for that same email address, **then** the SPA sends the pending Terms of Service and Privacy Policy acceptance records to `POST /users/policy-acceptances` and clears the pending browser copy after the API confirms success.
- **AC 4.6.8** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `GET /users/policy-acceptances/status`, **then** for each supported policy (`TermsOfService`, `PrivacyPolicy`) the API returns its current version, the newest version I have accepted (or `null`), and `needsAcceptance` (true when I have no acceptance row for the current version), plus a top-level `needsAcceptance` that is true when either policy needs it. Current versions live in one server-owned place (`CurrentPolicyVersions` in the Users feature); bumping one makes every account that has not accepted it need acceptance. The signup form's queued versions (`policyAcceptanceStorage.ts`) must equal them, which an API test enforces. Anonymous callers get **401**.
- **AC 4.6.9** [Authenticated] **Given** I am signed in and the status from AC 4.6.8 says a policy needs acceptance, **when** authentication has settled and any acceptance queued at signup (AC 4.6.7) has been sent, **then** a blocking dialog (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, labelled by its heading, focus moved to the heading and kept inside the dialog) asks me to accept: **"Please accept our Terms and Privacy Policy"** when I have never accepted either, otherwise **"We've updated our Terms and Privacy Policy"**, listing each policy that needs acceptance with its current version (and the version I accepted, if any), links to **Terms of Service** and **Privacy Policy**, **Accept and continue**, and **Sign out**. The dialog is the same for a missing record and a newer version. It is not shown on the legal pages (`/terms`, `/terms-of-service`, `/privacy`, `/privacy-policy`) so I can read them, nor on the transient `/auth/callback` landing, and returns when I leave them. **Accept and continue** sends `POST /users/policy-acceptances` with the current version of each policy that needs acceptance and the click time as `acceptedAtUtc`, then closes only after a fresh status check says nothing is needed; I am not asked again. If recording fails, the dialog stays open with an alert and a **Try again** action and never claims success. A brand-new account whose signup acceptance was recorded is not prompted; signed-out visitors are never checked. If the status check itself fails, no dialog is shown and the check runs again on the next sign-in or page load. The dialog itself fits within the viewport at 390px and at 200% zoom (640px CSS width).
- **AC 4.6.10** [Authenticated] **Given** my account was created through **Continue with Google** (no signup acceptance was queued), **when** I land on the destination after `/auth/callback`, **then** the AC 4.6.9 dialog ("Please accept our Terms and Privacy Policy") blocks until I accept, and the acceptance is recorded via `POST /users/policy-acceptances`. The dialog is not shown on `/auth/callback` itself.
- **AC 4.6.11** [Authenticated] **Given** my account exists only through Google, **then** I cannot add a password or use password recovery in this release; signing in with Google is the only way in until a password flow is added.
- **AC 4.6.12** [Anyone] **Given** I read the **Privacy Policy**, **then** its "Signing in with Google" paragraph states that Google shares basic profile information (such as name, email, verified status, profile picture and locale) and Quizymode keeps only the name, email and verified status, that the Google account identifier is stored by our identity provider (AWS Cognito), that this is used only to create or sign me in (and link it to a same-email account), that no contacts, Drive, Gmail or calendar data is accessed, and that it is not used for advertising; Google is listed among authentication providers. The Privacy Policy version is `2026-10-19` (`CurrentPolicyVersions.PrivacyPolicy` and `PRIVACY_POLICY_VERSION`), so every existing account sees the "We've updated our Terms and Privacy Policy" dialog once.

---

### AC 4.7 User settings

User settings are key-value pairs stored per user (e.g. **PageSize** for default pagination). Only the authenticated user can read or update their own settings. Other keys may be added over time (e.g. theme, language); validation and defaults are defined per key.

**API**

- **AC 4.7.1** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `GET /users/settings`, **then** the API returns 200 with a dictionary of my settings (key-value pairs); if I have no settings, an empty dictionary is returned.
- **AC 4.7.2** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `GET /users/settings`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 4.7.3** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `PUT /users/settings` with a JSON body `{ "key": "...", "value": "..." }`, **then** the API upserts that setting for my user (creates if missing, updates if present) and returns 200 with the key, value, and updatedAt. Key and value are required; key max length 100, value max length 500.
- **AC 4.7.4** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I call `PUT /users/settings`, **then** the API returns 401.
- **AC 4.7.4a** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `PUT /users/settings` with key `"StudyGuideMaxBytes"` and a numeric value in the range `0`–`1,000,000` (stored as a string), **then** that value becomes my effective study-guide byte limit; if the setting is absent, the default is **51,200 bytes (50 KB)**.
- **AC 4.7.4b** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I call `PUT /users/settings` with one of the admin-only study guide keys `StudyGuidePremium`, `StudyGuideDailySections`, or `StudyGuideMonthlySections` (any casing), **then** the API returns 400 (validation error "This setting can only be changed by an admin.") and stores nothing; only admins set these keys (AC 5.1.3b).

**Default pagination (PageSize)**

- **AC 4.7.5** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** my setting **PageSize** is set (via `PUT /users/settings` with key `"PageSize"` and a string value), **then** that value is used as my default number of items per page where the UI supports it (e.g. Categories list view). If not set, the default is 10. The UI and API may enforce a valid range (e.g. 1–1000); invalid values are clamped or rejected.
- **AC 4.7.6** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I use a page that supports pagination (e.g. Categories items list), **then** the default page size is 10 (no persisted setting).

**UI**

- **AC 4.7.7** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I open my profile, **then** I can view and change my default pagination (PageSize) via a dropdown with options 5, 10, 25, 50, 100; selecting a value persists it immediately via `PUT /users/settings` with no extra save step required.
- **AC 4.7.8** [Authenticated] **Given** I am on a page that uses default pagination (e.g. Categories items list, Collection detail), **when** the URL does not specify `pagesize`, **then** my saved PageSize setting is used; if the URL specifies `pagesize`, the URL value takes precedence for that page/session.
- **AC 4.7.10a** [Anyone] **Given** I am on the Collection detail page or Categories items list, **when** the per-page dropdown is shown, **then** the available options are 5, 10, 25, 50, 100; the Collection detail page initialises to the authenticated user's saved PageSize (or 10 for anonymous), and selecting a new value persists the change to user settings.
- **AC 4.7.9** [Authenticated] **Given** I see the content-compliance warning on add/import flows, **when** I choose **don't show again**, **then** the app persists that preference in `PUT /users/settings` and hides the warning on subsequent visits for that user.

**Show/hide answers toggle**

- **AC 4.7.11** [Anyone] **Given** I open the Categories items list or Collection detail list, **when** the list loads, **then** answers are shown by default (answer text visible on item cards with green highlight).
- **AC 4.7.12** [Anyone] **Given** I am in a list view (Categories items list or Collection detail), **when** the list is visible, **then** a compact **Show answers / Hide answers** toggle button appears in the control bar between the Filters button and the Map/Sort controls (Categories) or at the top of the item list (Collection detail). The toolbar renders as soon as the collection has at least one item, regardless of current page. On mobile only an eye icon is shown; on desktop the label "Show answers" or "Hide answers" is also displayed.
- **AC 4.7.13** [Anyone] **Given** answers are shown, **when** item cards render, **then** every answer text is shown with a light green background (`bg-green-100`) matching the correct-answer highlight style used in quiz mode.
- **AC 4.7.14** [Anyone] **Given** I toggle show/hide answers and then navigate to another list page without leaving list mode, **when** the new list loads, **then** the toggle retains its previous state (persisted in `sessionStorage` for anonymous users; persisted via `PUT /users/settings` key `HideAnswers` for authenticated users).
- **AC 4.7.14a** [Anyone] **Given** a list URL contains `?showAnswers=true` or `?showAnswers=false`, **when** the page loads, **then** the answer visibility is initialised from that parameter, overriding the user's stored preference for that visit. After mount the toggle continues to function normally and saves preferences as usual.
- **AC 4.7.15** [Authenticated] **Given** I am authenticated, **when** I toggle the answer visibility in a list view, **then** the setting is persisted as `HideAnswers` via `PUT /users/settings`; value `"true"` means hide, `"false"` means show. The default value (when the setting is absent) is `"false"` (shown).
- **AC 4.7.16** [Anonymous] **Given** I am not authenticated, **when** I toggle the answer visibility, **then** the preference is stored in `sessionStorage` only and reverts to shown on a new browser session.

**Content-compliance warning**

- **AC 4.7.10** [Authenticated] **Given** I open item creation or import flows (for example Create Item, Generate One AI Batch, Upload items to collection, or Study Guide prompt-set import), **when** I have not previously dismissed the warning, **then** the UI shows a warning reminding me not to upload infringing or unauthorized personal/confidential content, to review AI-generated content, and linking to the current Terms of Service and Privacy Policy.

---

### AC 4.8 Feedback submissions

Feedback submissions are lightweight inbound messages from the SPA. They may be sent by anonymous or authenticated users and are stored for later review.

**API**

- **AC 4.8.1** [Anyone] **Given** I call `POST /feedback` with a JSON body containing `type`, `currentUrl`, `details`, optional `email`, and optional `additionalKeywords`, **when** the payload is valid, **then** the API stores a feedback submission row and returns **201 Created** with the created submission payload.
- **AC 4.8.2** [Anyone] **Given** I call `POST /feedback` with an invalid `type`, an invalid or relative `currentUrl`, missing `details`, or an invalid optional email, **when** the request is processed, **then** the API returns **400 Bad Request** and does not create a submission.
- **AC 4.8.3** [Anonymous] **Given** I call `POST /feedback` anonymously, **when** the request succeeds, **then** the API accepts the submission without requiring authentication and stores no user id on the submission.
- **AC 4.8.4** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `POST /feedback` while authenticated, **when** the request succeeds, **then** the API associates the submission with my current app user id when available.
- **AC 4.8.5** [Anyone] **Given** I submit feedback of type **Provide feedback** or **Report issue**, **when** I include `additionalKeywords`, **then** the API ignores that field; only **Ask for more items** persists optional additional keywords.
- **AC 4.8.6** [Anyone] **Given** too many feedback submissions are sent from the same authenticated identity or client IP within a short time window, **when** the endpoint limit is exceeded, **then** the API returns **429 Too Many Requests** and rejects the submission to reduce abuse.

**UI**

- **AC 4.8.7** [Anyone] **Given** I fill out the feedback dialog and click **Submit**, **when** the request succeeds, **then** the dialog shows a success state without redirecting me away from the current page.
- **AC 4.8.8** [Anyone] **Given** feedback submission is temporarily blocked by the server rate limit, **when** I click **Submit**, **then** the UI shows an error explaining that I need to wait before trying again.

---

### AC 4.9 Ideas board

The ideas board is a public-facing product planning surface at `/ideas`. It shows published ideas grouped by lifecycle status and supports moderation for new submissions.

**API**

- **AC 4.9.1** [Anyone] **Given** I call `GET /ideas`, **when** the request succeeds, **then** the API returns only ideas whose moderation state is `Published`, and each idea includes the user-facing board fields: `title`, `problem`, `proposedChange`, optional `tradeOffs`, `status`, author/review metadata, rating summary, comment count, and permission booleans used by the SPA (`canEdit`, `canDelete`, and `canChangeStatus`; `canChangeStatus` is `true` only for admins and gates admin lifecycle/moderation controls — there is no separate `canModerate` flag).
- **AC 4.9.2** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `GET /ideas/mine`, **when** the request succeeds, **then** the API returns only ideas I created across `PendingReview`, `Published`, and `Rejected` moderation states so I can track my private submissions separately from the public board.
- **AC 4.9.3** [Authenticated] **Given** I call `POST /ideas` with `title`, `problem`, `proposedChange`, optional `tradeOffs`, and a valid Cloudflare Turnstile token, **when** the payload passes validation, **then** the API creates a new idea with `status = Proposed` and `moderationState = PendingReview`; the new idea is visible in my private submissions immediately but is not returned by `GET /ideas` until approved.
- **AC 4.9.4** [Authenticated] **Given** I submit an idea that is an exact normalized duplicate of one of my existing ideas, exceeds the daily quota, contains blocked profanity, or fails Turnstile verification, **when** the request is processed, **then** the API rejects it with an error (`409` for duplicate/quota conflicts, `400` for blocked content or verification/validation failures, `429` when the dedicated create rate limit is exceeded) and does not create a public idea row.
- **AC 4.9.5** [Owner/Admin] **Given** I call `PUT /ideas/{id}` for an idea I am allowed to edit, **when** the payload is valid, **then** the API updates the content fields, normalizes text, re-checks profanity/duplicates, and if a non-admin submitter edits a `Published` or `Rejected` idea the moderation state is moved back to `PendingReview` for re-review.
- **AC 4.9.6** [Owner/Admin] **Given** I call `DELETE /ideas/{id}` for an idea I am allowed to delete, **when** the request succeeds, **then** the API deletes the idea and its dependent idea-comments and idea-ratings.
- **AC 4.9.7** [Admin] **Given** I call `PUT /ideas/{id}/status`, **when** the payload contains one of `Proposed`, `Planned`, `In Progress`, `Shipped`, or `Archived`, **then** the API updates only the public lifecycle status of that idea; a missing, null, blank or any other value is rejected with `400` (request validation, or `Ideas.InvalidStatus` if it ever reaches the handler unparsed) and the stored status is left unchanged. Only `Published` ideas can change lifecycle status (`409 Ideas.StatusRequiresPublished` otherwise), and only these transitions are allowed (`409 Ideas.InvalidStatusTransition` for anything else, status unchanged): `Proposed` -> `Planned`/`In Progress`/`Archived`; `Planned` -> `Proposed`/`In Progress`/`Archived`; `In Progress` -> `Planned`/`Shipped`/`Archived`; `Shipped` -> `Archived`; `Archived` -> `Proposed`. Re-submitting the current status is a no-op success. The SPA only offers the allowed targets in the lifecycle select and disables it for unpublished ideas.
- **AC 4.9.8** [Anyone/Authenticated] **Given** an idea is `Published`, **when** I call `GET /ideas/{id}/comments`, **then** comments are readable publicly; **when** an authenticated user calls `POST /ideas/{id}/comments`, `PUT /ideas/{id}/comments/{commentId}`, or `DELETE /ideas/{id}/comments/{commentId}`, **then** create is allowed only on published ideas, comment create/update pass the moderation filter, comment create is rate-limited, only the comment owner may edit a comment, and the owner or any admin may delete a comment (so admins can remove spam/abuse on published ideas); `canDelete` is `true` for admins on every comment while `canEdit` stays owner-only.
- **AC 4.9.9** [Authenticated] **Given** an idea is `Published`, **when** I call `POST /ideas/{id}/rating` with `stars` from 1 to 5 or `null`, **then** the API upserts exactly one rating row per `(idea, user)` pair; unpublished ideas reject rating requests.
- **AC 4.9.10** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/ideas?moderationState={state}`, `POST /admin/ideas/{id}/approve`, or `POST /admin/ideas/{id}/reject`, **when** the request is valid, **then** the admin APIs list ideas by moderation state and allow approval to `Published` or rejection to `Rejected`; rejection requires `moderationNotes`; approval accepts an optional `moderationNotes` body field (max 1000 characters) that is stored as the approval note, and when it is omitted any existing moderation notes are preserved rather than cleared; moderation actions are recorded in audit logs.
- **AC 4.9.11** [System] **Given** the application starts after migrations, **when** bootstrap seeding runs, **then** a checked-in SQL script inserts curated idea rows idempotently (missing seeded ideas are added, existing edited ideas are left alone, and user-created ideas are never deleted).

**UI**

- **AC 4.9.12** [Anyone] **Given** I open `/ideas`, **when** the page loads, **then** I see a public board grouped in this fixed order: `Proposed`, `Planned`, `In Progress`, `Shipped`, `Archived`; the board is searchable across the visible idea text and supports `Recently updated` and `Top rated` sorting.
- **AC 4.9.13** [Anyone] **Given** I view an idea card on `/ideas`, **when** it is collapsed, **then** I see a compact summary with the title, problem snippet, proposed-change snippet, optional trade-offs snippet, status, rating summary, and comment count; **when** I expand it, **then** I see the full fields plus discussion.
- **AC 4.9.14** [Anonymous] **Given** I browse `/ideas` without signing in, **when** I view published ideas, **then** I can read ideas and comments but I am prompted to sign in before I can submit an idea, rate, or post a comment.
- **AC 4.9.15** [Authenticated] **Given** I am signed in on `/ideas`, **when** I click **Share an idea**, **then** I see a modal with `Title`, `Problem`, `Proposed change`, optional `Trade-offs`, and Turnstile verification; submitting the form sends the idea into moderation rather than publishing it immediately. The Turnstile widget is rendered once when the modal opens and stays in place while I type, and **Submit for review** becomes available once it returns a token. If the check fails, I see a **Try again** button that resets the widget without clearing what I typed, and the failure message disappears once a later check succeeds. Verification and any submit error (duplicate, rate limit, daily limit) sit in the modal footer next to **Submit for review**, so they stay visible without scrolling on short screens and phones while the form fields scroll above them.
- **AC 4.9.15a** [Authenticated] **Given** I fill in the idea form (create or edit), **when** a field has fewer meaningful characters (letters or digits, counted like the backend's `CountMeaningfulCharacters`) than the backend minimum — `Title` 3, `Problem` 10, `Proposed change` 10, and `Trade-offs` 10 when provided — **then** the modal shows an inline error such as "Title must contain at least 3 letters or digits." and does not submit, so punctuation/whitespace-only input like `---` never round-trips to an `Ideas.*TooShort` server error.
- **AC 4.9.15b** [Authenticated] **Given** I write or edit an idea comment, **when** I type, **then** I see a `{trimmed length}/2000` counter; while the trimmed text is empty, has fewer than 3 letters or digits, or exceeds 2000 characters, **Post comment** / **Save** stays disabled and (for non-empty text) an inline error is shown, mirroring the backend `IdeaComments.TextTooShort` rule.
- **AC 4.9.16** [Authenticated] **Given** I am signed in on `/ideas`, **when** I view **My submissions**, **then** I see my own ideas grouped by `Pending review`, `Published`, and `Rejected`, including moderation notes for rejected or flagged ideas; unpublished ideas in this panel do not expose public comments/ratings.
- **AC 4.9.17** [Admin] **Given** I open `/admin/ideas`, **when** the page loads, **then** I see moderation tabs for `Pending review` (selected by default), `Published`, `Rejected`, and `All` (which calls `GET /admin/ideas?moderationState=all` and lists every idea with its moderation badge), plus controls to approve (with an optional note), reject with a required note, edit, delete, and update lifecycle status on each idea.
- **AC 4.9.12a** [Anyone/Authenticated/Admin] **Given** I call `GET /ideas`, `GET /ideas/mine`, or `GET /admin/ideas`, **when** I pass optional `page` (default 1) and `pageSize` (default 50, clamped to 1-100), **then** the response returns only that page (stable order with an id tiebreaker) plus `page`, `pageSize`, and `totalCount`; out-of-range values are clamped rather than rejected. The SPA board, My submissions, and `/admin/ideas` queue show Previous/Next controls with "Page X of Y" only when more than one page exists, reset to page 1 when the admin moderation tab changes, step back a page if the current page empties, and apply search/sort to the loaded page.
- **AC 4.9.15c** [Authenticated] **Given** an idea or comment action fails with a known Ideas error, **when** the SPA shows the error, **then** it uses specific copy instead of the generic `[status] detail` banner: `Ideas.Duplicate` (already submitted this content), `Ideas.DailyLimitExceeded` (daily limit reached, try tomorrow), `Ideas.ProfanityBlocked` / `IdeaComments.ProfanityBlocked` (reword and retry), `Ideas.InvalidStatusTransition` / `Ideas.StatusRequiresPublished` (lifecycle change not allowed), and any HTTP `429` (slow down and wait a few minutes, matching the 10-minute idea, comment and rating limit windows); unknown errors keep the generic message.
- **AC 4.9.18** [Anyone] **Given** a seeded idea is shown in the ideas UI, **when** its stored author is the internal seeder identity, **then** the displayed author name is `Quizymode` rather than the raw seeding account value.

## 5. Admin

### AC 5.0 Admin: database size monitoring

**API**

- **AC 5.0.1** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/database/size`, **then** the API returns the current PostgreSQL database size in bytes/MB/GB, usage percentage against the 500 MB free-tier budget, exact total counts for `Items` and `Keywords`, and a `topTables` list containing the 5 largest user tables ordered by total relation size.

**UI**

- **AC 5.0.2** [Admin] **Given** I open `/admin/database-size`, **when** the page loads, **then** I see the current database size summary, the total item count, the total keyword count, and a visible list of the top 5 largest tables by size so I can quickly spot where storage is concentrated.

---

### AC 5.1 Admin: per-user study guide settings

**API**

- **AC 5.1.1** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/users/{id}/settings` for a valid user ID, **then** the API returns 200 with a dictionary of that user's settings (key-value pairs); if the user has no settings, an empty dictionary is returned; if the user does not exist, the API returns 404.
- **AC 5.1.2** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `PUT /admin/users/{id}/settings` with a JSON body `{ "key": "...", "value": "..." }` for a valid user ID, **then** the API upserts that setting for the specified user (creates if missing, updates if present) and returns 200 with the key, value, and updatedAt; if the user does not exist or the request is invalid, an error is returned (404 or 400).
- **AC 5.1.3** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin and I call `PUT /admin/users/{id}/settings` with key `"StudyGuideMaxBytes"` and a numeric value in the range 0–1,000,000 (as a string), **when** the request succeeds, **then** that value becomes the per-user study guide byte limit for that user; if the setting is absent, the default limit of 51,200 bytes (50 KB) applies.
- **AC 5.1.3b** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `PUT /admin/users/{id}/settings` with key `StudyGuidePremium` (`"true"` / `"false"`), `StudyGuideDailySections`, or `StudyGuideMonthlySections` (whole number `0`–`100000` as a string, or `""` to clear the override), **then** the API stores the value under the canonical key casing (value trimmed); any other value returns 400. `StudyGuidePremium = "true"` puts the user on the premium study guide tier and the overrides replace that tier's daily / monthly section limits (AC 6.6.8).
- **AC 5.1.3a** [Admin] **Given** I want to inspect one user’s tracked browsing activity, **when** I call `GET /admin/users/{id}/page-view-history` with optional filters such as days, urlContains, page, and pageSize, **then** the API returns that user’s details plus an activity summary for the selected window and a paged URL history grouped by exact URL, including how many times each URL was opened and the first/last open time in the selected window; if the user does not exist, the API returns 404.

**UI**

- **AC 5.1.4** [Admin] **Given** I am on the Admin Dashboard, **when** I click **Users & Activity**, **then** I am taken to an admin users page where I can browse registered users instead of having to load one by ID manually. On desktop and mobile the page never scrolls horizontally: a wide users table scrolls inside its own card and the user detail panel stays fully visible.
- **AC 5.1.5** [Admin] **Given** I am on the admin users page, **when** the page loads, **then** I see summary cards for registered-user counts, filters for search and recent activity, and a paged list of registered users showing at least email/name, registration time, unique URLs opened in the selected window, and last opened time.
- **AC 5.1.6** [Admin] **Given** I click a user in the admin users list, **when** their detail panel opens, **then** I see that user’s basic details, a grouped URL history with open counts and first/last open times for the selected window, the existing study-guide limit editor, and the **Study guide plan** panel (AC 5.1.8) for that user.
- **AC 5.1.7** [Admin] **Given** I have a user selected in the admin users page, **when** I edit the **StudyGuideMaxBytes** value (or leave it blank for default) and click **Save**, **then** the UI calls the admin user-settings API to upsert the `"StudyGuideMaxBytes"` setting for that user; after a successful save, the effective limit (bytes and KB) reflects the new value.
- **AC 5.1.8** [Admin] **Given** I have a user selected in the admin users page, **when** I use the **Study guide plan** panel, **then** I can check **Premium** (setting `StudyGuidePremium` = `"true"`/`"false"`) and enter optional **Daily sections override** / **Monthly sections override** (`StudyGuideDailySections` / `StudyGuideMonthlySections`, whole numbers 0–100,000, validated with Zod before saving; blank = plan default, sent as `""` to clear an existing override); the panel shows the plan defaults (free 10/day, 60/month; premium 50/day, 1,000/month) and the effective limits, **Save plan** is disabled until something changed and upserts only the changed keys through `PUT /admin/users/{id}/settings`, then shows "Study guide plan saved."

---

### AC 5.2 Admin: repo-managed seed sync for items and public collections

**Design:** Admin seed sync reads canonical repo files directly from GitHub at a specific ref instead of accepting a pasted/uploaded manifest. The runtime request supplies repository owner/name, git ref, and the API fetches the pre-built items bundle plus public collection files from GitHub. It validates the resulting item and collection set, then previews or applies idempotent upserts by permanent `itemId` (`Items.Id`) and `collectionId` (`Collections.Id`). Categories and navigation still use taxonomy slugs (`category`, `navigationKeyword1`, `navigationKeyword2`), not database keyword IDs. Missing items or collections are **not** deleted implicitly; deleting or consolidating repo-managed content remains an explicit maintenance action.

**API**

- **AC 5.2.1** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `POST /admin/seed-sync/preview` with a JSON body containing `schemaVersion`, `repositoryOwner`, `repositoryName`, `gitRef`, and optional `deltaPreviewLimit`, **then** the API fetches the canonical items bundle and public collection seed files from GitHub, validates the loaded item and collection set, and returns 200 with source metadata (repo/ref/resolved commit/items path/collections path), item summary counts, collection summary counts, and a `changes` array containing only delta items (not unchanged items), capped by `deltaPreviewLimit`.
- **AC 5.2.2** [Admin] **Given** I call preview or apply with invalid input (for example duplicate `itemId` or `collectionId` values in the loaded source, an unsupported `schemaVersion`, an invalid taxonomy category, an invalid navigation path, an `itemId` that already belongs to a private or non-repo-managed row, or a collection that references a missing item), **when** the request is processed, **then** the API returns 400 Bad Request with a validation or contract error and does not mutate data. Seed items with 0 or more than 4 incorrect answers, or any blank incorrect answer, are also invalid input (400, nothing changed) (#250). Seed items with duplicate incorrect answers, or an incorrect answer equal to the correct answer, are also invalid input (400, nothing changed) (#276). A loaded manifest whose items or collections list is missing, a collection seed file without `itemIds`, or a seed item without `incorrectAnswers` is likewise invalid input: the API returns 400 `Admin.SeedSyncManifestInvalid` with the "X is required." validation message, never an internal exception message, and changes nothing (#273). A manifest whose items or collections list contains a null entry returns 400 `Admin.SeedSyncManifestInvalid` with `Items must not contain null entries.` / `Collections must not contain null entries.` and changes nothing (#281).
- **AC 5.2.3** [Admin] **Given** I call `POST /admin/seed-sync/apply` with an item whose `itemId` already exists on a public repo-managed row, **when** the source-controlled content differs, **then** the API **updates** that existing DB row in place (question, answers, explanation, source, navigation, and extra keywords) instead of creating a duplicate item. **Contrast with user bulk-create:** `POST /items/bulk` always inserts new rows and never persists a caller-supplied repo identity.
- **AC 5.2.4** [Admin] **Given** I call `POST /admin/seed-sync/apply` with an item whose `itemId` does **not** exist in the current database (for example because the row was manually deleted earlier), **when** the request succeeds, **then** the API creates a new public repo-managed item using that explicit `itemId` and marks the row with `IsRepoManaged = true`.
- **AC 5.2.5** [Admin] **Given** an admin seed-sync item includes extra keywords beyond the navigation pair, **when** a keyword name does not already exist as a public keyword, **then** the API creates it as a **public** keyword (not private pending) and attaches it to the repo-managed item; navigation keywords are also attached to the item keywords like other item ingress flows.
- **AC 5.2.6** [Admin] **Given** some existing public repo-managed rows are absent from the GitHub-loaded item set for the requested path/ref, **when** I call preview or apply, **then** the API leaves those rows untouched; current apply behavior does **not** infer deletes from missing rows.
- **AC 5.2.6a** [Admin] **Given** the GitHub-loaded public collection set contains a `collectionId` that already exists on a repo-managed public collection, **when** the source-controlled name, description, or item membership differs, **then** `POST /admin/seed-sync/apply` updates that existing collection in place instead of creating a duplicate.
- **AC 5.2.6b** [Admin] **Given** the GitHub-loaded public collection set contains a `collectionId` that does **not** exist in the current database, **when** the request succeeds, **then** the API creates a new public repo-managed collection using that explicit `collectionId`, links the referenced items, and marks the row with `IsRepoManaged = true`.
- **AC 5.2.6c** [Admin] **Given** some existing public repo-managed collections are absent from the GitHub-loaded collection set for the requested ref, **when** I call preview or apply, **then** the API leaves those collection rows untouched; current apply behavior does **not** infer deletes from missing collections.
- **AC 5.2.7** [Admin] **Given** I call `POST /admin/seed-sync/apply` and the request succeeds, **when** the API commits the sync, **then** it also persists exactly one seed-sync run history row containing at least the repo/ref/commit source metadata, summary counts, timestamp, and the triggering admin user id when available.
- **AC 5.2.8** [Admin] **Given** a successful seed-sync apply affects items, **when** the run history is stored, **then** the API also persists one per-item history row for each affected item containing at least `itemId`, action (`Created`, `Updated`, or `Deleted`), and a changed-fields summary; unchanged items do not create per-item history rows.

**UI**

- **AC 5.2.9** [Admin] **Given** an admin UI is built on top of the seed-sync API, **when** it requests a preview, **then** it should show the returned delta rows and summary counts rather than trying to render the full GitHub-loaded item set.
- **AC 5.2.10** [Admin] **Given** I am on the Seed Sync screen, **when** I enter a GitHub repository and git ref, **then** the UI sends that repo/ref request to the API instead of uploading a manifest JSON payload; the screen shows both item and public-collection sync counts from the API response.
- **AC 5.2.11** [Admin] **Given** canonical seed-source files in GitHub contain extra fields that are not part of the seed-sync item schema (such as `isPrivate`, `factualRisk`, `reviewComments`, or editor-only arrays like `factChecks` with optional nested fields such as `source` URLs), **when** the API loads those files, **then** those extra fields are silently ignored; repo-managed items are always public (`IsPrivate = false`) regardless of any `isPrivate` value in source. **Note:** Some repo-managed exam items may append an `UPD260411:` line to the human-readable `explanation` string (plain text) to record a documentation excerpt and URL; that suffix is persisted like any other explanation text. Editorial passes may also rewrite the prose before that suffix (for example replacing generic filler or correcting a mismatched keyed answer) while keeping `itemId` stable for seed sync.
- **AC 5.2.12** [Admin] **Given** I am using seed sync for a production change, **when** I choose a GitHub ref, **then** I should prefer an immutable commit SHA over a branch head so the preview/apply result is reproducible and auditable.
- **AC 5.2.13** [Admin] **Given** I am on the Seed Sync screen, **when** the page loads, **then** I see a recent sync-history summary sourced from persisted seed-sync run history rather than only the transient result of the last browser action.
- **AC 5.2.14** [Admin] **Given** I apply a seed sync from the Seed Sync screen, **when** the request succeeds, **then** the apply summary shows that the run was recorded in persisted history and the recent-history panel refreshes to include that new run.
- **AC 5.2.15** [Admin/System] **Given** the item registry is generated by `scripts/build_item_registry.py`, **when** the script runs, **then** it produces `data/seed-source/_registry/source-file-index.json` — a flat index of all 866+ source files with `path`, `category`, `nav1`, `nav2`, `itemCount`, and `modifiedAt` (last git commit date); no duplicate shard files or bundle are generated; the original `items/` source files remain the single source of truth.
- **AC 5.2.16** [Admin] **Given** I call `POST /admin/seed-sync/preview` or `POST /admin/seed-sync/apply` with a `sinceCommitSha`, **when** the backend resolves the current commit, **then** it fetches `source-file-index.json` (~212 KB), calls the GitHub compare API to identify changed source file paths, downloads only those original source files from `data/seed-source/items/`, and processes only the items they contain; items already in the DB are not re-examined.
- **AC 5.2.17** [Admin] **Given** I call the apply endpoint without `sinceCommitSha` (full sync), **when** the request includes `fileOffset` and `fileBatchSize`, **then** the backend processes the slice `files[fileOffset : fileOffset + fileBatchSize]` of the source-file index, returns `isComplete = false` with `nextFileOffset` when more files remain, and returns `isComplete = true` on the final batch; the frontend loops until complete.
- **AC 5.2.17a** [Admin/System] **Given** repo-managed item source JSON is maintained under `data/seed-source/items`, **when** files are created, merged, renamed, or chunked, **then** each file name follows `<category>.<nav1>.<nav2>.json` for sets with 100 or fewer items, or `<category>.<nav1>.<nav2>.<chunk>.json` with zero-padded chunk suffixes (`001`, `002`, ...) for larger sets split into chunks of at most 100 items. Files must not use ad hoc small numeric suffixes (for example `.2.json`) when the set can fit in one semantic file. Moving or chunking items must preserve the existing `itemId` for the same logical repo-managed item.
- **AC 5.2.18** [Admin] **Given** the Seed Sync UI has sync history, **when** the page loads, **then** an "Incremental sync" toggle is shown pre-checked, with the last successful commit SHA pre-filled as `sinceCommitSha`; when the toggle is off, the UI displays a full-sync warning and a progress bar showing files processed during batched apply.
- **AC 5.2.19** [Admin/System] **Given** `ApplyManifestAsync` is called with any number of items, **when** it processes changes, **then** it flushes `SaveChangesAsync` every 200 items within the open transaction rather than accumulating all entity changes and saving once; this prevents memory and timeout issues at scale.
- **AC 5.2.20** [System/SEO] **Given** `scripts/build_item_registry.py` runs in CI, **when** it completes, **then** it also regenerates `src/Quizymode.Web/public/sitemap.xml` with three tiers of category URLs derived from `source-file-index.json`: (a) `/categories/{cat}` at priority 0.8 with `lastmod` = max of all L2 dates under that category, (b) `/categories/{cat}/{nav1}` at priority 0.7 with `lastmod` = max of all L2 dates under that L1, (c) `/categories/{cat}/{nav1}/{nav2}` at priority 0.6 with `lastmod` from the source file's last git commit date; static app pages are preserved with their fixed dates.
- **AC 5.2.21** [System/SEO] **Given** a Quizymode link is shared on a social platform or chat app, **when** a link-preview crawler reads the page, **then** the static `index.html` and the `SEO` component both point `og:image` and `twitter:image` at the raster `https://www.quizymode.com/og-image.png` (1200×630, under 300 KB) and include `og:image:type` (`image/png`), `og:image:width` (`1200`), `og:image:height` (`630`), `og:image:alt`, and `twitter:image:alt`; no production tag references `og-image.svg`, which stays in `public/` only as the design source. The deploy workflow uploads `og-image.png` with `Content-Type: image/png` and a one-day cache. Preview tags set by `SEO` after load are not seen by crawlers (they do not run JavaScript), so per-page preview images are out of scope.

---

### AC 5.3 Admin: usage analytics and page-hit reporting

**Design:** SPA page-hit analytics are captured from client-side route changes so both anonymous and authenticated visits are recorded even when navigation happens without a full page reload. Each page-hit stores the app-relative URL (`path` plus optional query string), a client-generated session id, the resolved client IP, the request user agent, a bot flag derived from that user agent, whether the visitor was authenticated at the time of the hit, and the current app user id when available. Admin reporting has two views: a **page view** (headline totals, top pages, recent hits) and a **session view** that rolls hits up by session id to distinguish visitors who landed and left from those who stayed and explored.

**Session definitions:**

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Session** | All page hits sharing a `SessionId` within the selected window. The session id lives in `sessionStorage`, so it is scoped to a browser tab and resets when that tab closes; the same person in two tabs is two sessions. |
| **Bounce** | A session with exactly **1** page hit. |
| **Engaged session** | A session with **2 or more distinct paths**. |
| **Duration** | `MAX(CreatedUtc) − MIN(CreatedUtc)` for the session. A bounce is always `0`: no exit event is recorded, so dwell time on the final page is unknowable. Averages and medians are computed over non-bounce sessions only. |
| **Outcome** | Exactly one of `anonymous` (never authenticated), `converted` (first hit anonymous, a later hit authenticated), or `authenticated` (first hit already authenticated). Classification is by **first hit**, so a mid-session logout does not reclassify the session. |

**API**

- **AC 5.3.0** [System] **Given** a page hit is recorded, **when** `POST /analytics/page-views` stores the row, **then** the request `User-Agent` header is persisted (truncated to 512 characters, stored as null when absent) and an `IsBot` flag is computed from it at write time by case-insensitive substring match against a known list of crawler, scraper, headless-browser, link-unfurler, and uptime-monitor markers. A missing or blank user agent is **not** classified as a bot, so page-hit rows recorded before user-agent capture existed are not retroactively reclassified.

**API**

- **AC 5.1.0** [Admin] **Given** I need to review registered users in the admin area, **when** I call `GET /admin/users` with optional filters such as search text, activity window days, activity filter, page, and pageSize, **then** the API returns a paged list of registered users with at least id, name, email, createdAt, lastLogin, unique URL count in the selected activity window, total page views in that window, and last opened time; the response also includes summary counts for total registered users, filtered users, and users with/without activity in the selected window.

- **AC 5.3.1** [Anyone] **Given** the SPA calls `POST /analytics/page-views` with a JSON body containing `path`, optional `queryString`, and `sessionId`, **when** the payload is valid, **then** the API stores a page-hit row for that URL, accepts both anonymous and authenticated callers, records the client IP from forwarded headers / remote address, and associates the hit with the current app user when available. The database write is not cancelled if the client disconnects mid-request (uses `CancellationToken.None` for the save).
- **AC 5.3.2** [Anyone] **Given** the SPA sends an invalid page-hit payload (for example missing or blank `sessionId`, blank `path`, a path that does not start with `/`, or an oversized URL component), **when** `POST /analytics/page-views` is processed, **then** the API returns **400 Bad Request** and does not create a page-hit row.
- **AC 5.3.3** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/page-view-analytics` with optional filters such as `days`, `visitorType`, `pathContains`, `page`, `pageSize`, `topPagesLimit`, and `includeBots`, **then** the API returns 200 with: (1) summary totals for hits, unique pages, unique sessions, authenticated hits, anonymous hits, bot sessions, and the selected window; (2) a top-pages list grouped by path and ordered by most visited; and (3) a paged recent-hit list that includes full URL, session id, IP address, visitor type, timestamp, and user email when the hit is tied to an authenticated user.
- **AC 5.3.3a** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/page-view-analytics`, **when** `includeBots` is omitted or false, **then** hits flagged as bot traffic are excluded from every figure in the response; the count of bot sessions in the window is still reported in the summary (with `botsExcluded = true`) so the size of the excluded share stays visible.
- **AC 5.3.3b** [Admin] **Given** a session begins anonymous and later authenticates, **when** the analytics summary is computed, **then** that session is counted exactly once — classified by its first hit, so it falls under `anonymousSessions` — and `authenticatedSessions + anonymousSessions` always equals `uniqueSessions`. Per-hit `authenticatedPageViews` and `anonymousPageViews` continue to reflect each individual hit.
- **AC 5.3.4** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/page-view-analytics` with an unsupported `visitorType` or an invalid `days` value, **when** the request is processed, **then** the API returns **400 Bad Request** with a validation error and does not return a partial report.
- **AC 5.3.7** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/page-view-sessions` with optional `days`, `outcome`, `minPages`, `includeBots`, `sortBy`, `page`, `pageSize`, and `landingPagesLimit`, **then** the API groups page hits by session id and returns 200 with: (1) a summary containing total sessions, total hits, bounced sessions, bounce rate, engaged sessions, average pages per session, average and median duration over non-bounce sessions, bot session count, and a per-outcome breakdown; (2) a session-depth histogram bucketed as 1 / 2 / 3-5 / 6-10 / 11+ pages, each split by outcome; (3) a landing-page list with sessions, bounces, bounce rate, and average depth per entry path; and (4) a paged session list with outcome, start and end time, page hits, distinct pages, duration, entry and exit path, IP, resolved country, user email, user agent, and bot flag.
- **AC 5.3.7a** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/page-view-sessions` with `outcome` or `minPages`, **when** the report is built, **then** those two filters narrow the returned **session list only**; the summary, depth histogram, and landing pages continue to describe every session in the window so the denominators (and therefore the bounce rate) stay stable while drilling in.
- **AC 5.3.7b** [Admin] **Given** the selected window contains more than 50,000 sessions, **when** `GET /admin/page-view-sessions` is processed, **then** the API returns **400 Bad Request** asking for a shorter time range rather than materializing an unbounded result set. An invalid `days`, `outcome`, or `sortBy` value likewise returns **400 Bad Request**.
- **AC 5.3.8** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/page-view-sessions/{sessionId}`, **then** the API returns 200 with the session's outcome, timing, totals, visitor detail, and every page hit in chronological order, each carrying a step number, URL, authentication state at that step, timestamp, and dwell time measured to the next step; the **final step's dwell time is null** because no exit event is recorded. An unknown session id returns **404 Not Found** and a blank session id returns **400 Bad Request**.
- **AC 5.3.13** [System] **Given** a page hit is recorded, **when** `POST /analytics/page-views` stores the row, **then** it sets `IsInternal = true` when the caller is an admin (Cognito admin group), when the authenticated user has `Users.IsInternal = true`, or when the body carries `internalBrowser: true` (the client-asserted internal-browser marker). Trusting the marker is acceptable because it can only remove the sender's own traffic from reports.
- **AC 5.3.14** [Admin] **Given** a session contains **any** internal hit, or any hit tied to a user currently flagged internal, **then** the whole session is internal — including anonymous hits recorded before the visitor signed in. The user flag is joined at query time, so flagging a user removes their past sessions from reports without rewriting rows, and unflagging restores them.
