- **AC 5.3.15** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/page-view-analytics`, `GET /admin/page-view-sessions`, `GET /admin/page-view-sessions/{sessionId}`, `GET /admin/users`, or `GET /admin/users/{id}/page-view-history`, **when** `includeInternal` is omitted or false, **then** every hit belonging to an internal session is excluded from every figure. Summaries report `internalSessions` (internal sessions in the window, counted even when excluded, mirroring the bot count) and `internalExcluded`. With `includeInternal=true` they are included and session rows carry `isInternal`. An internal session's detail lookup returns **404 Not Found** unless `includeInternal=true`. `GET /admin/users` also returns each user's `isInternal` flag and an `internalUsers` count; the user list itself is never filtered by the flag, so an internal user can always be found and unflagged.
- **AC 5.3.16** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `PUT /admin/users/{id}/internal` with `{ "isInternal": true|false }`, **then** the user's flag is updated and the change is written to the audit log as `UserInternalFlagChanged` (a no-op repeat writes nothing). A missing `isInternal` or invalid id returns **400**, an unknown user **404**, and a non-admin caller **403** (Admin policy).
- **AC 5.3.21** [System] **Given** a page hit is for `/search` (with or without a trailing slash), **when** `POST /analytics/page-views` stores the row, **then** the server drops the whole query string and any `?` or `#` suffix on `path`, so the text a visitor searched for is never stored whichever field the client put it in (AC 11.13). The SPA strips it before sending too.

**UI**

- **AC 5.3.5** [Anyone] **Given** I navigate within the SPA, **when** the route changes and the app is ready to evaluate the current auth state, **then** the frontend records the current page hit using a stable session id for the browser tab/session and avoids obvious duplicate sends caused by immediate remount/re-render behavior.
- **AC 5.3.6** [Admin] **Given** I am on the Admin Dashboard, **when** I click **Usage Analytics**, **then** I am taken to an admin report page with **Sessions** and **Pages** tabs, a shared time-window selector, and a shared "Include bots and crawlers" toggle that is off by default. The **Pages** tab retains the existing view: visitor-type filter, path-text filter, summary cards, top pages, traffic mix, and recent URL hits with session ids and IP addresses.
- **AC 5.3.9** [Admin] **Given** I open the **Sessions** tab, **then** I see headline cards for bounce rate, engaged sessions, average pages per session, median time on site, and conversions; a per-outcome engagement table; a session-depth histogram stacked by outcome; a landing-page table that highlights entry paths with a bounce rate at or above 70%; and a session list I can filter by outcome, sort by recency, page count, or duration, and narrow to multi-page sessions only via "Hide bounces".
- **AC 5.3.10** [Admin] **Given** bot traffic was excluded and the window contains bot sessions, **when** I view the Sessions tab, **then** a note reports how many bot sessions were excluded and explains that crawlers typically view a single page, so including them inflates the bounce rate.
- **AC 5.3.11** [Admin] **Given** I expand a row in the session list, **then** the session's ordered journey is fetched at that moment (not with the list) and each step shows its URL, timestamp, whether the visitor was signed in at that step, and how long they stayed; the final step reads "last page — dwell unknown" rather than reporting zero.
- **AC 5.3.12** [Admin] **Given** a session's page hits predate user-agent capture, **when** it is displayed, **then** its client is labelled "Unknown UA" rather than "Human", so pre-migration rows are not presented as verified human traffic.
- **AC 5.3.17** [Anyone] **Given** I am on any `/admin` or `/admin/*` route, **then** the SPA records no page hit for it. **Given** this browser carries the internal-browser marker (`localStorage` key `quizymode.analytics.internal-browser` = `"true"`), **then** every page hit it sends includes `internalBrowser: true`, whether or not I am signed in. If storage is unavailable the browser is treated as unmarked.
- **AC 5.3.18** [Admin] **Given** I am on **Usage Analytics**, **then** an **Exclude this browser from analytics** checkbox sets or clears the internal-browser marker for the current device, and an **Include internal traffic** toggle (off by default) applies to every tab (Pages, Sessions, and Funnel). When internal sessions were excluded, each tab notes how many (bot and internal counts are reported independently, so a session that is both appears in each); when included, internal session rows show an **Internal** badge and their journey lookup opts into internal traffic.
- **AC 5.3.19** [Admin] **Given** I am on **Users & Activity**, **then** internal users show an **Internal** badge, the user detail panel has an **Internal user (exclude from analytics)** checkbox that calls `PUT /admin/users/{id}/internal` and refreshes the list and history, and an **Include internal traffic** toggle (off by default) controls whether activity counts and URL history include internal sessions.
- **AC 5.3.20** [System] **Given** Playwright runs any project (setup, screenshots, smoke, or full E2E), **then** every browser context receives the internal-browser marker through an init script in `playwright/fixtures.ts` before any page script runs — including specs that start from an empty `storageState` — so runs against production produce only internal sessions.


### AC 5.4 Conversion event funnel (FUNNEL-001)

**Design:** Engagement events are stored in their own `AnalyticsEvents` table, separate from page hits, and only for an allowlist of names. Each row carries bounded context fields only: event name, the page-view `sessionId`, the user id when authenticated, the app-relative path (query string and fragment stripped), an optional context type and id, and optional run fields (`runId`, `score`, `total`; `itemId`, `position`, `isCorrect` are reserved for study events, ANALYTICS-007). The server assigns the timestamp and computes a bot flag from the user agent. Question text, answer text, chosen options, emails, tokens, full external referrers, and arbitrary payloads are never accepted. The Privacy Policy (effective 2026-10-15, recorded as policy version `2026-10-15` at sign-up; superseded by 2026-10-16, AC 5.6) discloses these engagement events and the browser user-agent used for bot filtering.

**Run/event contract (shared with ANON-002 #108 and ANALYTICS-007 #127):** a run is identified by a client-generated `runId` (at most 64 chars, `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]`). For a run-scoped event the API derives the idempotency key `{event}:run:{runId}` (plus the item id for item-level events) and enforces it with a unique `(SessionId, DedupeKey)` index, so **one run can store at most one `quick_quiz_started` and one `quiz_completed` per session**, whatever the client retries or re-renders. **Play again** must use a new `runId`. The first completion stored for a run is kept; later duplicates are ignored.

**API**

- **AC 5.4.1** [Anyone] **Given** the SPA calls `POST /analytics/events` with `sessionId`, 1–25 `events`, and optional `internalBrowser`, **when** every event is valid, **then** the API stores them for anonymous or authenticated callers (associating the user when authenticated) and returns **202 Accepted** with `{ accepted, duplicates }`. Like page hits (AC 5.3.13), events are stored with `IsInternal = true` when the caller is an admin, is a user flagged internal, or sends `internalBrowser: true`; the SPA sends the marker from browsers marked internal. Allowlisted names are `quick_quiz_started`, `quiz_completed`, `signup_prompt_viewed`, `signup_prompt_clicked`, `signup_form_started`, `signup_submitted`, `signup_confirmation_shown`, `signup_confirmed`, `sign_in_completed`, `guest_state_imported`, and the study-engagement events `question_answered`, `flashcards_started`, `flashcard_revealed`, `flashcard_graded` (AC 5.5.16) and `quiz_result_shared` (AC 5.5.17) (AC 5.5.1), and the global-search events `search_performed` and `search_result_clicked` (AC 5.4.11).
- **AC 5.4.2** [Anyone] **Given** any event in the batch has an unknown name, a path that is not app-relative or exceeds 512 characters, a context type outside `auth`, `category`, `collection`, `daily`, `flashcards`, `home`, `item`, `learn`, `mine`, `quiz`, `review`, `search`, a context id without a type or longer than 128 characters or with characters outside `[A-Za-z0-9._:/-]`, a `clientEventId` or `runId` longer than 64 characters, a field the event does not accept, or a missing required field, **then** the API returns **400** with field-level validation errors and stores nothing. `quick_quiz_started` requires `runId` (optional `total`); `quiz_completed` requires `runId`, `score`, and `total` with `0 ≤ score ≤ total ≤ 500`; `flashcard_graded` requires `runId`, `itemId`, `position` and `isCorrect` and rejects `score` and `total`; `quiz_result_shared` requires `runId`, `score` and `total` with `score ≤ total` and rejects `itemId`, `position` and `isCorrect`; the signup and sign-in events accept no run fields. The study-engagement events add their own required fields — see AC 5.5.1.
- **AC 5.4.3** [Anyone] **Given** an event repeats an earlier event's idempotency key in the same session (same run for run-scoped events, or same `clientEventId` for others), including within one batch, **then** it is counted under `duplicates` and not stored again. The same `runId` in a different session is stored separately. Events with neither a `runId` nor a `clientEventId` are not deduplicated.
- **AC 5.4.4** [Anyone] **Given** a caller exceeds 120 requests in a 10-minute window (partitioned by user subject when authenticated, otherwise client IP), **then** `POST /analytics/events` returns **429**. A batch counts as one request.
- **AC 5.4.5** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/analytics/funnel` with optional `days` (1–365, default 7), `includeBots`, and `includeInternal`, **then** the API returns, for each funnel step in the order listed in AC 5.4.1, the unique sessions and total events in the window, the conversion from the previous step, and the conversion from the first step (both null when the denominator is zero, and null from-previous for the first step), plus the number of sessions with any event and the bot-session count. Steps are counted independently, so a later step may exceed an earlier one. Bot sessions are excluded unless `includeBots=true`. An invalid `days` returns **400**; a non-admin caller gets **403**.
- **AC 5.4.5a** [Admin] **Given** any of a funnel session's events was stored as internal, or the session shares its session id with an internal page-view session (an admin, a user flagged internal, or a browser marked internal; see AC 5.3.13–5.3.20), or any of its events belongs to a user currently flagged internal, **when** I call `GET /admin/analytics/funnel` without `includeInternal=true`, **then** the whole session is left out of every step and of `sessionsWithEvents`. The response reports `internalSessions` and `internalExcluded`. The user flag is evaluated at query time, so flagging a user removes their earlier funnel sessions too.
- **AC 5.4.11** [Anyone] **Given** `POST /analytics/events` carries `search_performed`, **then** it requires `total` (0-500, the number of results shown; zero is valid) and accepts no other run field; `search_result_clicked` requires `position` (1-500, the 1-based rank of the clicked result) and accepts no other run field. Both may carry a `contextType` (`search`, `category`, `collection`, `item`, or `mine`) and a `contextId` for a public target only. Neither event has a field for the search text, so it cannot be sent, and neither is a funnel step (AC 5.4.5 is unchanged).

**UI**

- **AC 5.4.6** [System] **Given** frontend code calls `trackEvent`, **then** events queued in the same tick are sent as one batch (at most 25 per request) with the page-view session id and the current app-relative path. Delivery never throws, never blocks the user action, and is not retried on failure.
- **AC 5.4.7** [System] **Given** frontend code records an impression with `trackEventOnce(key, …)` or `useTrackEventOnce`, **then** the event is sent at most once per tab session for that key, including across React StrictMode double effects, remounts, and reloads in the same tab; a short token key is also sent as `clientEventId` so the API dedupes retries.
- **AC 5.4.8** [Admin] **Given** I open **Usage Analytics**, **then** a **Funnel** tab lists each step with its label, event name, unique sessions with a relative bar, total events, and conversion from the previous and first step (shown as "—" when undefined), using the shared time window, bot toggle, and internal-traffic toggle, and notes how many bot and internal sessions were excluded.
- **AC 5.4.9** [Anyone] **Given** I start a quiz run in quiz mode (including after **Play again**), **then** the SPA records one `quick_quiz_started` for that run with its `runId` and question count; **when** I answer every question, **then** it records one `quiz_completed` with the same `runId`, my score, and the total. Both carry where the quiz came from: `home` for the home page's 5-question quiz, `collection` or `category` with its id (omitted when the id falls outside the API's context-id rule), `daily` with the UTC date as its id for the daily quiz (AC 3.13.6), `review` for review-queue runs (AC 3.14.4), or `quiz` for unscoped quizzes. Restoring a run in the same tab, remounts, and retries do not record it again. For a Test (AC 3.18), `quick_quiz_started` is sent on **Start test** (not when the setup card appears) and `quiz_completed` on submit or timeout.
- **AC 5.4.10** [Anyone] **Given** I use the signup and sign-in pages, **then** the SPA records these funnel events, each at most once per tab session (AC 5.4.7) so retries, resends and remounts do not inflate counts: `signup_form_started` when I first focus the signup form; `signup_submitted` when a signup attempt passes local validation and is sent on; `signup_confirmation_shown` when the identity provider accepted the signup and the code step appears (not when the step is restored after a reload); `signup_confirmed` when my confirmation code is accepted, even if automatic sign-in then fails; `sign_in_completed` when a verified authenticated session exists after signing in or after confirming a new account. Failed attempts record nothing past `signup_submitted`. Every event carries context type `auth` and, only when the sign-up reason is one of the allowlisted values (AC 4.2), that reason as the context id; it never carries an email, display name, password, confirmation code or return path. Events are sent in the same tab session as my earlier quiz events, so the funnel joins them across the anonymous-to-signed-in change. When a contextual signup prompt (AC 4.2.10) is shown, the SPA records `signup_prompt_viewed`, and when I follow its **Sign up** action it records `signup_prompt_clicked`; each at most once per tab session per reason, with context type `auth` and the prompt's reason as the context id only (no return path, score, or user data). After a signed-in guest-progress claim (AC 3.6.16) the server confirms it saved at least one run, the SPA records `guest_state_imported` (context type `auth`, no context id, counts, or run data), at most once per tab session; a failed or fully rejected import records nothing. A completed Google redirect sign-in (AC 4.3.10) records `sign_in_completed` under the same once-per-tab-session rule; there are no new event names.

---

### AC 5.5 Study engagement events and reporting (ANALYTICS-007)

**Design:** A quiz run's start and end are already `quick_quiz_started`/`quiz_completed` (AC 5.4.9) — a quiz run is not double-counted as a separate "run started" event. `question_answered` is the item-level event within that same `runId`. A Flashcards run has no API start/end counterpart of its own: the SPA creates one client-generated `runId` per page visit and scope (category, collection, or the whole bank), and reports `flashcards_started` once items load plus one `flashcard_revealed` per card the first time its answer is shown. All four study events share the run/event contract in AC 5.4 (idempotency key, one row per run/session, `Play again` or a new scope gets a new `runId`).

**API**

- **AC 5.5.1** [Anyone] **Given** `POST /analytics/events` carries a `question_answered` event, **then** it requires `runId`, `itemId` (a GUID), `position` (1–500, the item's 1-based position in the run), and `isCorrect`; the dedupe key adds the item id, so **one run stores at most one `question_answered` per item**, keeping only the first value received for a given item even if a later, different answer to the same item is sent. `flashcards_started` requires `runId` (optional `total` = card count) and follows the same one-per-run-per-session rule as `quick_quiz_started`. `flashcard_revealed` requires `runId`, `itemId`, and `position`, deduped per item like `question_answered`. Violating any of these field rules returns **400** per AC 5.4.2.
- **AC 5.5.2** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/analytics/learning` with optional `days` (1–365, default 7), `includeBots`, `includeInternal`, `channel` (`paid`, `email`, `campaign`, `search`, `social`, `referral`, `direct`, or `unknown`, from the session's first-hit attribution; sessions without attribution are `unknown`), and `visitorType` (`new` for a visitor's first session, `returning` for later ones; sessions without a visitor id match neither), **then** the API groups study events into runs by `(sessionId, runId)` — a run counts only when its start event (`quick_quiz_started` or `flashcards_started`) is present — and returns, per `(mode, contextType, contextId)` scope: runs started, runs completed and completion rate (quiz only; null for Flashcards), average score percent over completed quiz runs, and average items reached (questions answered or cards revealed per started run). It also returns, for quiz runs only, the count of runs that started and never answered a question, and a drop-off list by question position with how many started runs reached that position (and the resulting share percent) and how many unfinished runs stopped exactly there. Bot sessions are excluded unless `includeBots=true`, internal sessions unless `includeInternal=true` (reported the same way as AC 5.4.5/5.4.5a). An invalid `days` returns **400**; a non-admin caller gets **403**. A started Test that is never submitted reports no answers (its `question_answered` events are sent at submit only, AC 3.18.11), so the report counts it as a run that never answered a question.

**UI**

- **AC 5.5.3** [System] **Given** frontend code records a high-volume per-item event with `trackEventDeferredOnce(key, …)`, **then** the event is held in memory (deduped per key for the page load) rather than sent immediately, and is flushed — in batches of 5 — when a 5th event accumulates, when the caller explicitly flushes (a run's completion, or the page unmounting), or when the page is hidden (the `pagehide` event, or `visibilitychange` turning `hidden`). The page-hide path sends a `keepalive` `fetch` carrying the bearer token to `${API_URL}/analytics/events` directly, because `navigator.sendBeacon` cannot send a JSON body with an `Authorization` header to the API's origin.
- **AC 5.5.4** [Anyone] **Given** I am answering questions in quiz mode, **then** in Practice mode the SPA calls `trackQuestionAnswered` only for the **first** answer I give to each question (AC 5.4.9's score already reflects only the first answer; later changes to the same question record nothing); answers are held and sent with the batching/flush behavior in AC 5.5.3, and any still held are flushed when I leave the quiz page. In Test mode it calls it once per answered question at submit (manual or timeout), with the correctness of the final answer and the roster position; unanswered questions send nothing, and the events are sent before `quiz_completed` (AC 3.18.11).
- **AC 5.5.5** [Anyone] **Given** I open Flashcards for a category, collection, or the whole bank, **then** the SPA starts one Flashcards run (a fresh `runId`) the first time items finish loading for that page visit and scope, records `flashcards_started` with the card count, and records `flashcard_revealed` the first time each card's answer is shown, using the same batching/flush behavior as quiz answers; navigating within the same scope does not start a new run, but revisiting the page, changing scope, **Restart deck** and toggling **Shuffle** (**AC 3.14.8**, **AC 3.14.10**) do.
- **AC 5.5.6** [Admin] **Given** I open **Usage Analytics**, **then** a **Learning** tab shows a table of study runs by mode and scope (runs started, runs completed, completion %, average score %, average items reached) and a quiz drop-off list by question position with a relative bar and how many runs stopped at each position, using the shared time window, bot toggle, and internal-traffic toggle, and notes how many bot and internal sessions were excluded. **Channel** and **Visitor type** (all, new, returning) selectors above the table apply the AC 5.5.2 filters to both the table and the drop-off list and stay visible while loading or after a failure.
- **AC 5.5.7** [Anyone] **Given** I complete a 10-question quiz, **then** the SPA sends exactly one `quick_quiz_started`, ten `question_answered` (positions 1–10, one per item even if I change an answer) and one `quiz_completed`, all with the same `runId`, in a few batched requests well inside the AC 5.4.4 budget; **given** I close or hide the tab after four answers, **then** those four answers are delivered by the page-hide path (AC 5.5.3) and the Learning report counts the run as stopped at position 4 (AC 5.5.2); **given** I choose **Play again**, **then** the new run has a new `runId` and its answers are recorded again.
- **AC 5.5.8** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/analytics/content` with optional `days` (1-365, default 30), `includeBots`, and `includeInternal`, **then** the API returns, for the window: per category (page-hit scope key), navigation set (`category/keyword/keyword`), and collection - sessions, study sessions (Quiz or Flashcards page hits in that scope), item steps, ratings count and average stars (item ratings attributed through the item's category, navigation keywords, and collection memberships; collection ratings for collections), comments, collection bookmarks and shares, and completed quiz runs with average score where the study-engagement report has runs for that scope; featured performance per `FeaturedItem` (sessions that viewed home or `/featured`, how many of those later opened that entry's category/navigation set or collection in the same session, and the click-through percent, counting a session once per entry); public categories and public collections with no Quiz or Flashcards page hit in the window (private content is excluded); and user-created items (private versus public) and collections created per week (Monday start), excluding repo-managed content. Bot and internal sessions are excluded and reported as in AC 5.5.2. An invalid `days` returns **400**; a non-admin caller gets **403**.
- **AC 5.5.9** [Admin] **Given** I open **Usage Analytics**, **then** a **Content** tab shows tables for categories, navigation sets, and collections, a featured click-through table, the untouched public categories and collections, and user-created content by week, using the shared time window, bot toggle, and internal-traffic toggle.
- **AC 5.5.10** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/analytics/question-quality` with optional `days` (1-365, default 30), `minAnswers` (1-10000, default 10), `includeBots`, and `includeInternal`, **then** the API returns items ordered by correct rate, lowest first (at most 200), each with the question, correct answer, category, collections it belongs to, first-attempt answer count, correct rate, random-guess rate (100 divided by one correct plus the incorrect answers), a `checkAnswerKey` flag (true only when the correct rate is strictly below the random-guess rate), and the item's last edit time. A first attempt is the earliest `question_answered` event from each session for the item; only answers recorded after the item's last `UpdatedAt` count, so editing an item resets its statistics. Items with fewer first-attempt answers than `minAnswers` are not listed. Bot and internal sessions are excluded and reported as in AC 5.5.2. Invalid `days` or `minAnswers` returns **400**; a non-admin caller gets **403**.
- **AC 5.5.11** [Admin] **Given** I open **Usage Analytics**, **then** a **Questions** tab shows the question quality table with an adjustable minimum-answers input (default 10), the label "since last edit", a "Check answer key" badge on flagged items, and an **Edit** link to the item edit page, using the shared time window, bot toggle, and internal-traffic toggle.
- **AC 5.5.12** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/analytics/lifecycle` with optional `weeks` (1-52, default 12), **then** the API returns weekly signup cohorts (Monday start, from `Users.CreatedAt`, internal users excluded, each user counted once) with: signups; users activated within 7 days (a completed quiz, `quiz_completed`, by the user within 7 days of signup) and how many have reached that 7-day mark; users active in week 1, week 2, and week 4 after signup (at least one page view on a UTC day in `[signup day + 7N, signup day + 7N + 7)`) with the eligible count for each, counting a user as eligible only once that whole window has ended. It also returns a signup-source table by first-touch channel and campaign with signups, activated, and week 1 retention. A user's source is the first session of the visitor id (`AnalyticsSession.VisitorId`) carried by their sessions; with no visitor id the channel of their own earliest session is used; with no session it is `unknown`. An invalid `weeks` returns **400**; a non-admin caller gets **403**.
- **AC 5.5.13** [Admin] **Given** I open **Usage Analytics**, **then** a **Lifecycle** tab shows the cohort table and the signup-source table with active/eligible counts and percentages.
- **AC 5.5.14** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/users`, **then** each user also carries sessions and study sessions in the activity window, collections and items created (excluding repo-managed content), ratings, comments, and signup channel (AC 5.5.12); `sortBy` (`lastActive` default, `sessions`, `studySessions`, `collections`, `items`, `ratings`, `comments`, `channel`, `registered`) with `sortDir` (`asc` or `desc`, default `desc`) orders the whole filtered list, and `activityFilter=dormant` returns users with no page view in the last 30 days (a user with no page views at all is judged by signup date). An unknown `sortBy` or `activityFilter` returns **400**.
- **AC 5.5.15** [Admin] **Given** I open **Users and Activity** and select a user, **then** the users table shows the new columns with sortable headers (`aria-sort`) and a **Dormant (30d)** filter, and the user detail lists the user's sessions (via `GET /admin/page-view-sessions?userId=`) each expandable to its journey, alongside the grouped URL history.
- **AC 5.5.16** [Anyone] **Given** I grade a Flashcards card, **then** the SPA sends `flashcard_graded` with `runId`, `itemId`, `position` (1-based in the full deck) and `isCorrect` (true = Got it), all required (missing fields return **400** per AC 5.4.2; `score` and `total` are rejected). The Flashcards event context is used, guests send it too, and only the first grade per card per run is stored (server dedupe on run and item). The event is not a funnel step.
- **AC 5.5.17** [Anyone] **Given** a quiz result share succeeds (native share resolves or copy succeeds), **then** the client sends one `quiz_result_shared` per run id with `runId`, `score` and `total`, plus the run's own context. Daily runs use `contextType: "daily"` and review runs never send it. The API accepts the event only with all three of `runId`, `score` and `total` and `score <= total`, rejects `itemId`, `position` or `isCorrect` with 400 per AC 5.4.2, and stores at most one per run per session. The event is not a conversion funnel step.

---

### AC 5.6 Front-end error reporting (OBS-001)

**Design:** Browser crashes are stored in their own `ClientErrors` table and logged as structured Serilog warnings (so they reach Grafana Loki next to API logs). No third-party SDK, session replay, automated source-map decoding, or alerting. Each row holds bounded, sanitized fields only: source (`boundary`, `window`, `promise`), error name (100 chars), message (500), stack (4000), component stack (2000), app-relative path without query string or fragment (512), app version, the page-view `sessionId`, an optional `visitorId` (the returning-visitor id of AC 5.7.3, sent by the SPA unless Global Privacy Control withholds it), the user id when authenticated, the user agent, a device class (`desktop`, `mobile`, `tablet`, `unknown`), a bot flag, the internal flag (same rule as AC 5.3.13), a server timestamp, and a fingerprint. Before storing, the server removes query strings and fragments from absolute URLs and from app-relative paths (for example `/signup?code=…` becomes `/signup`), bare `?key=value` tokens, URL userinfo, email addresses, JWTs and bearer tokens from every text field. The **fingerprint** is the first 32 hex characters of SHA-256 over the error name, the message with bundle content hashes removed and GUIDs, hex ids and numbers normalized (so a stale-chunk `ChunkLoadError` groups across releases), and the top stack frame without line/column numbers or bundle content hashes, so one bug groups across sessions and releases. Production builds use `sourcemap: "hidden"`: maps are generated but not referenced by bundles, are excluded from every S3 sync, and are published as a CI artifact per version (`web-sourcemaps-<version>-<run>`, and `deployed-web-sourcemaps-<sha>` for the deployed build). The Privacy Policy (effective 2026-10-19, recorded as policy version `2026-10-19` at sign-up; 2026-10-16 disclosed diagnostic error reports, 2026-10-17 added the visitor identifier to them and 2026-10-19 adds Google sign-in) discloses diagnostic error reports.

**API**

- **AC 5.6.1** [Anyone] **Given** the SPA calls `POST /client-errors` with a JSON body (sent as `application/json`, or as `text/plain` by `navigator.sendBeacon`) carrying `source`, `message`, `path`, `sessionId`, and optional `name`, `stack`, `componentStack`, `appVersion`, `visitorId`, `internalBrowser`, **then** the API stores one sanitized row for anonymous or authenticated callers and returns **202** with `{ stored, reason, fingerprint }`. Overlong text fields are truncated to the limits above, never rejected. A missing or blank `name` is stored as `Error`.
- **AC 5.6.2** [Anyone] **Given** the body is not JSON, `source` is not one of the three values, `message` is blank, `path` is not app-relative (must start with `/`, not `//`), `sessionId` is missing, longer than 128 characters or outside `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]`, `visitorId` breaks the same rule, or `appVersion` is longer than 32 characters or outside `[0-9A-Za-z.+-]`, **then** the API returns **400** and stores nothing. A body over 32 KiB returns **413** and stores nothing.
- **AC 5.6.3** [Anyone] **Given** a report has the same fingerprint as one already stored for that session, **then** it is not stored again (`stored: false`, `reason: "duplicate"`), enforced by a unique `(SessionId, Fingerprint)` index so concurrent duplicates also store one row; **given** the session already has 10 stored reports, **then** a new one is not stored (`reason: "session_limit"`). Only stored reports are logged.
- **AC 5.6.4** [Anyone] **Given** a caller exceeds 30 requests in a 10-minute window, **then** `POST /client-errors` returns **429** and stores nothing. The window is partitioned by user subject when authenticated, otherwise by the server-trusted client IP (`Connection.RemoteIpAddress`): a client-sent `X-Forwarded-For` value or `sessionId` never selects the partition, so rotating them buys no budget. Forwarded hops are trusted only from proxies listed in `ForwardedHeaders:KnownNetworks` (comma-separated CIDRs; loopback in Development); without it, anonymous reports behind a proxy share the proxy's partition. **Given** 300 reports were already stored by all callers in the last 10 minutes, **then** the API returns **429** and stores nothing, whatever the session id or IP.
- **AC 5.6.5** [System] **Given** a report is stored, **then** the API writes a Serilog warning with the properties `ClientErrorFingerprint`, `ClientErrorName`, `ClientErrorSource`, `ClientErrorPath`, `ClientAppVersion`, and `ClientErrorMessage`.
- **AC 5.6.6** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/client-errors` with optional `days` (1–365, default 7), `appVersion`, `includeBots`, and `includeInternal`, **then** the API groups the window's reports by fingerprint, most frequent first, with error name and message, source, report count, affected sessions, first seen (earliest report ever within retention) and last seen, whether the group is new (first seen inside the window), latest app version, the top 5 paths, browser families and device classes with counts, and the latest report's stack, component stack and session id. It also returns totals (`totalReports`, `totalGroups`, `newGroups`), the app versions present in the window, the bot report count, and the internal session count. Bot reports are excluded unless `includeBots=true`; internal sessions (any report stored internal, a page-view session that is internal, or a user currently flagged internal; as in AC 5.4.5a) are excluded unless `includeInternal=true`. At most the 10,000 most recent reports are grouped (`truncated` says when more existed) and at most 100 groups are returned. An invalid `days` or `appVersion` returns **400**; anonymous callers get **401** and non-admin callers **403**.
- **AC 5.6.7** [System] **Given** the API is running, **then** a daily background job deletes `ClientErrors` rows older than 90 days.

**UI**

- **AC 5.6.8** [System] **Given** the SPA starts, **then** it listens for window `error` events (capture phase, so failed `<script>`, module-preload and stylesheet loads are reported as `ChunkLoadError`) and `unhandledrejection` events, and the root `ErrorBoundary` reports every render error it catches with React's component stack. A rejection whose reason is not an `Error`, string, or error-like object is reported only as `Non-Error promise rejection (<type>)`; its value is never serialized. Cross-origin `Script error.` events with no detail, and errors whose stack frames all come from browser extensions, are ignored.
- **AC 5.6.9** [System] **Given** an error is reported, **then** the SPA sends each distinct error (same name, message, and top stack frame) at most once per tab session and at most 10 reports per tab session, including across reloads in the same tab (state in `sessionStorage` key `quizymode.client-errors.sent`, with an in-memory fallback). Before sending it truncates fields to the API limits, strips query strings, fragments, emails and tokens, and sends the app-relative path without its query string, `__APP_VERSION__`, the page-view session id, and `internalBrowser: true` from browsers carrying the internal marker (AC 5.3.17). Anonymous reports go by `navigator.sendBeacon` (a `text/plain` body, no preflight), falling back to `fetch` with `keepalive`; signed-in reports use a `keepalive` `fetch` with the bearer token, because a beacon cannot carry it. Reporting never throws, never blocks the UI, and is never retried.
- **AC 5.6.10** [Anyone] **Given** a render error reaches the root error boundary, **then** I see a "Something went wrong" page with the error details and a **Reload Page** button, whether or not the report could be sent.
- **AC 5.6.11** [Admin] **Given** I open **Admin → Client Errors** (`/admin/client-errors`), **then** I see the totals and one card per error group with the fields from AC 5.6.6, a collapsible sample stack and component stack, and a **Show sample session journey** button that loads that session's page hits (or says none were recorded). I can filter by time window (24 hours, 7, 30, or 90 days), app version, and include bots or internal traffic; by default both are excluded and the excluded counts are noted. Non-admins are redirected home. The admin overview's work queue also shows the count of new error groups for its window (AC 5.10.3), linking here.

### AC 5.7 Traffic attribution and returning visitors (ANALYTICS-002 #122, ANALYTICS-003 #123)

**Design:** A new `AnalyticsSessions` table holds one first-hit attribution snapshot per page-view `sessionId`: landing path/query, referrer host (never the full referrer URL), UTM tags, which ad platform's click-id was present (never the click-id value itself), a resolved channel, device class, best-effort country, and the optional visitor id. The snapshot is written once, on the first `POST /analytics/page-views` seen for a session, and is never modified by later hits in that session.

- **AC 5.7.1** [System] **Given** `POST /analytics/page-views` is called with a `sessionId` that has no existing `AnalyticsSessions` row, **then** the API creates one, deriving `channel`, `referrerHost`, `clickIdPlatform`, and the UTM columns from the optional `referrer`, `utmSource`, `utmMedium`, `utmCampaign`, `utmContent`, `utmTerm`, `gclid`, `gbraid`, `wbraid`, `msclkid`, and `ttclid` fields on the request, `deviceClass` (`mobile`, `tablet`, `desktop`, or `unknown`) from the request's `User-Agent`, `appVersion` from the optional `appVersion` field (truncated to 32 characters), `landingPath`/`landingQuery` from the hit's path and query string, `visitorId`/`userId` when available, and `country` best-effort (AC 5.7.6). A concurrent first hit for the same brand-new session that loses a unique-`sessionId` race is discarded without error or overwrite (mirrors the dedupe-race handling in AC 5.4.3).
- **AC 5.7.2** [System] **Given** a session already has an `AnalyticsSessions` row, **then** every subsequent `POST /analytics/page-views` for that `sessionId` leaves every attribution column on that row unchanged — only first-hit values are ever stored.
- **AC 5.7.3** [System] **Given** `POST /analytics/page-views` or `POST /analytics/events` is called with a `visitorId`, **then** the API uses it only when it is a syntactically valid UUID; an invalid value is silently ignored (treated as absent) and the hit or events are still stored as normal — a malformed visitor id never rejects the request. When the session's `AnalyticsSessions` row already exists with no visitor id, a later page hit or event batch with a valid one fills it in.
- **AC 5.7.3a** [System] **Given** a session's row has no `userId` and a later page hit or event batch in that session is authenticated, **then** the row's `userId` is set to that user, so the anonymous visits of the same visitor id connect to the account. Only `userId` and `visitorId` are ever filled in later; attribution columns stay first-hit only.
- **AC 5.7.4** [System] **Given** the request's referrer, UTM tags, and click-id params, **then** the channel is resolved by the first matching rule, in order: (1) **paid** — any of `gclid`, `gbraid`, `wbraid`, `msclkid`, `ttclid` is present, or `utmMedium` is `cpc`, `ppc`, `paid`, `paid_social`, `paidsocial`, `display`, or `cpm`; (2) **email** — `utmMedium` is exactly `email`; (3) **campaign** — any other `utm_*` tag is present; (4) **search** — the referrer's host matches a known search engine (Google, Bing, DuckDuckGo, Yahoo, Baidu, Yandex); (5) **social** — the referrer's host matches a known social platform (Reddit, Facebook, Instagram, X/Twitter, t.co, LinkedIn, YouTube, TikTok, Pinterest, Discord, Hacker News); (6) **referral** — any other non-empty external referrer host; (7) **direct** — no referrer and no tags. `fbclid` is never treated as a paid signal on its own (Facebook appends it to organic shares too), so a bare Facebook referrer with no UTM or ad click-id classifies as `social`, not `paid`.
- **AC 5.7.5** [System] **Given** a referrer URL, **then** only its host is extracted and stored (never the full URL, path, or query string), truncated to 255 characters; a referrer whose host matches the current request's own host is treated as no referrer (`null`, channel falls through toward `direct`). Each UTM value is independently truncated to 100 characters rather than rejected when oversized. A click-id param maps to a platform name only (`gclid`/`gbraid`/`wbraid` → `google`, `msclkid` → `microsoft`, `ttclid` → `tiktok`) — the raw click-id value is never persisted in any column or written to logs.
- **AC 5.7.6** [System] **Given** a first hit for a session, **then** country resolution via `IIpGeolocationService` is best-effort and non-blocking: it is bounded by a short internal timeout and any failure (timeout, network error, lookup miss) is caught and stored as `null` country rather than delaying or failing the page-view response.
- **AC 5.7.7** [System] **Given** the API starts up, **then** a one-time backfill (batches of 5,000, idempotent) creates an `AnalyticsSessions` row for every pre-existing `PageViews` session id that has none, using that session's earliest hit: `firstSeenUtc`/`landingPath`/`landingQuery` from that hit, UTM tags parsed from its stored query string, `deviceClass` from its stored user agent (`unknown` when the hit predates user-agent capture), and `channel` resolved with no referrer — which can only ever resolve to `direct` or `campaign` for backfilled rows, never `search`/`social`/`referral`/`paid`, since no referrer or click-id data exists for history. `referrerHost`, `country`, and `visitorId` are always `null` for backfilled rows. A failed backfill run is logged and never blocks API startup; it retries on the next deploy.
- **AC 5.7.8** [Admin] **Behavior change — internal-traffic exclusion now also propagates between sessions sharing a visitor id.** In addition to the existing own-evidence rule (AC 5.3.14: a session is internal if any of its own hits is internal, or is tied to a user currently flagged internal), a session with a non-null `visitorId` is now also treated as internal if **any other** `AnalyticsSessions` row with that same non-null `visitorId` is internal by its own evidence. Two sessions that both have a `null` `visitorId` never match each other this way — the join requires both sides' `visitorId` to be non-null. This can change which sessions `GET /admin/page-view-sessions`, `GET /admin/page-view-analytics`, `GET /admin/users`, and related reports exclude by default, for any visitor who has at least one internal session (for example, an admin on one device whose personal browser later matches the same visitor id).
- **AC 5.7.9** [System] **Given** the SPA tracks a page view or sends engagement events, **then** it includes an optional `visitorId`: a random UUID created on first use and stored in `localStorage` under `quizymode.analytics.visitor-id` (unlike the tab-scoped session id, this persists across tabs and reloads, so reloads and new tabs reuse it while each tab still gets its own session id; clearing site data produces a new visitor). The SPA never creates, reads, or sends it when `navigator.globalPrivacyControl === true`; a stored value that is not a syntactically valid UUID is treated as absent and replaced. If `localStorage` is unavailable, no visitor id is sent (no per-page-load id is generated). The page hit is recorded either way. Each page hit also sends the SPA build version as `appVersion`.
- **AC 5.7.10** [System] **Given** a tab loads the SPA, **then** before the router mounts the app captures `document.referrer` and the `utm_source`/`utm_medium`/`utm_campaign`/`utm_content`/`utm_term`/`gclid`/`gbraid`/`wbraid`/`msclkid`/`ttclid` values from the initial `location.search` into `sessionStorage`, so the values survive the URL rewrite that `QuizModePage`/`ExploreModePage` perform after mount. The page-view tracker includes whatever was captured (and the visitor id) on every hit it sends in that tab; the API only uses them when creating a session's first-hit snapshot (AC 5.7.1–5.7.2).
- **AC 5.7.11** [Admin] **Given** I open **Admin → Usage Analytics → Acquisition**, **then** `GET /admin/analytics/acquisition?days=&includeBots=&includeInternal=&deviceClass=&country=` returns sessions grouped by channel, each with sessions, bounce rate, engaged rate (2+ distinct pages), converted sessions (started anonymous, then signed in), and conversion rate, plus a source/campaign breakdown that I can expand per channel, and by-device and by-country breakdowns. Bounce, engaged, and converted use the same session classification as the Sessions tab, so the total equals the Sessions-tab total for the same window and exclusions. Bot and internal sessions (including AC 5.7.8 visitor propagation) are excluded by default and their counts are noted. The device and country filters narrow the whole report. Sessions with no attribution row are grouped under `unknown`. `days` outside 1–365 returns **400**; non-admin callers get **403**.
- **AC 5.7.12** [Admin] **Given** I view the Sessions tab or the Acquisition tab, **then** both show a **New vs Returning Visitors** panel computed by the same code: new-visitor sessions (the visitor's first-ever session), returning-visitor sessions (the visitor had an earlier session, possibly before the window), sessions without a visitor id (counted as neither), and distinct new and returning visitors. The Acquisition tab also shows, for first-time visitors in the window grouped by their first session's channel and campaign, how many came back with another session within 7 days and the return rate.
- **AC 5.7.13** [Admin] **Given** an account was created in the window and is linked to a visitor id (AC 5.7.3a), **then** the panel reports signups with visit history, the median and average number of pre-signup sessions (that visitor's sessions that started before the account was created and never linked to it), and the median and average UTC calendar days from the visitor's first session to account creation. Example: anonymous visits on two different days and a signup on a third day count as 2 pre-signup sessions and 2 days to signup. Bot and internal exclusions apply; the device and country filters do not.
- **AC 5.7.14** [Anyone] **Given** I read the Privacy Policy, **then** it describes the referrer domain, campaign tags, ad-platform click-id presence (never the value), device class, and approximate country recorded per session, and the browser visitor identifier, its purpose, that it is not created under Global Privacy Control, and how to clear it.


### AC 5.8 Feature and scope classification for page hits (ANALYTICS-004 #124)

**Design:** A server-side classifier (`PageHitClassifier`) runs at write time in `POST /analytics/page-views` and stores five columns on each `PageViews` row: `Area` (feature area), `ScopeType` (`category`, `collection`, `item`, or null), `ScopeKey` (category slug, collection id, or item id; lower-cased; at most 200 characters), `NavPath` (category plus up to two navigation keywords from `/categories/:category/:kw1/:kw2` path segments or the comma-separated `nav` query parameter; category scope only), and `IsItemStep` (true when a Quiz or Flashcards path contains `/item/<id>`, i.e. one question viewed). Specific routes are matched before parameterised ones, and unknown paths map to `other`.

| Route | Area |
| --- | --- |
| `/` | `home` |
| `/categories*`, `/items` | `browse` |
| `/quiz*` | `quiz` |
| `/explore*` | `flashcards` |
| `/collections*` | `collections` |
| `/featured` | `featured` |
| `/items/:id`, `/items/:id/comments` | `item-detail` |
| `/create*` (including `/create/notes`, which previously counted as `study-guide` under `/study-guide/upload`), `/items/add`, `/items/bulk-create`, `/items/upload`, `/items/create`, `/add-new-item`, `/items/:id/edit` | `create` |
| `/study-guide*` | `study-guide` |
| `/search` (exactly) | `search` |
| `/login`, `/signup` | `auth` |
| `/ideas`, `/roadmap`, `/feedback` | `community` |
| `/about`, `/privacy*`, `/terms*` | `info` |
| `/admin*` | `admin` |
| anything else | `other` |

- **AC 5.8.1** [System] **Given** `POST /analytics/page-views` stores a hit for `/quiz/geography/item/<id>?nav=capitals`, **then** the row has `Area = quiz`, `ScopeType = category`, `ScopeKey = geography`, `NavPath = geography/capitals`, `IsItemStep = true`. **Given** `/explore/collections/<id>/<slug>/item/<id>`, **then** `Area = flashcards`, `ScopeType = collection`, `ScopeKey = <collection id>`, `IsItemStep = true`. **Given** `/create` or `/items/add`, **then** `Area = create`. **Given** a path matching no route, **then** `Area = other`.
- **AC 5.8.2** [System] **Given** the API starts, **then** a background backfill classifies every `PageViews` row whose `Area` is still null with the same classifier (batches of 2,000; idempotent; a failed run is logged and retried on the next start), so backfilled rows match newly written rows exactly. Until a row is backfilled, reports count it as `other` in SQL aggregates and classify it on the fly where rows are already in memory (landing groups and journey steps).
- **AC 5.8.3** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/page-view-analytics`, **then** the response also includes `featureAreas` (per area: hits, distinct sessions, share of sessions as a percentage of `uniqueSessions`, item steps) whose `hits` add up to `summary.totalPageViews`, and `contentScopes` with `topCategories`, `topNavPaths`, and `topCollections` (each limited by `topPagesLimit`; per row: key, sessions, hits, item steps, and average item steps per session), with collection names resolved from `Collections` (null when the collection no longer exists). The raw `topPages` list is unchanged. All existing filters (window, visitor type, path contains, bots, internal) apply.
- **AC 5.8.4** [Admin] **Behavior change —** `GET /admin/page-view-sessions` groups `landingPages` by the entry hit's area, scope type, and scope key instead of by exact entry path; each row carries `area`, `scopeType`, `scopeKey`, and `path` (the most common entry path in the group, as an example). The summary adds `studySessions` (sessions with at least one Quiz or Flashcards item step) and `avgItemStepsPerStudySession`; each listed session adds `entryArea` and `itemSteps`. `GET /admin/page-view-sessions/{sessionId}` adds `area` and `isItemStep` to every journey step.
- **AC 5.8.5** [Admin] **Given** I open **Usage Analytics → Pages**, **then** a **Group by** switch offers **Feature area** (default; a table of sessions, hits, and share of sessions per area), **Content scope** (top categories, navigation sets, and collections with sessions, item steps, and average item steps per session; collections show their name, or "Unknown collection"), and **Raw URL** (the previous most-visited-pages list). Summary cards and the recent-hits table are shown in every mode.
- **AC 5.8.7** [System] **Given** `POST /analytics/page-views` stores a hit for `/search`, **then** the row has `Area = search` and no scope; `/search/anything` is `other`. The new area appears in `featureAreas` like any other.
- **AC 5.8.2a** [System] **Behavior change (ANALYTICS-011) —** the classification backfill runs as the first step of every page-hit maintenance pass (AC 5.9.1) rather than as its own startup service, so it never races the rollup or pruning steps.
- **AC 5.8.6** [Admin] **Given** I open the **Sessions** tab, **then** cards show **Sessions with study activity** and **Avg questions / study session**, the landing table lists entry area and scope with an example path, and each expanded journey step is labelled with its area.

### AC 5.9 Page-hit retention and daily rollups (ANALYTICS-011 #131)

**Decisions:** raw `PageViews` hits are kept **180 days**. `AnalyticsSessions` snapshots are **not** kept longer than raw hits: a snapshot is deleted once it is older than the same cutoff and its session has no hits left (keeping it longer would lose the hit-based internal-traffic evidence that reports rely on, AC 5.3.14). Aggregates go to a `PageViewDailyRollups` table, kept indefinitely, whose rows carry no session, visitor, user, IP, or user-agent identifiers. Grain: UTC day x area x scope type x scope key (category or collection only; item scope is dropped to bound row count) x channel x device class x country x outcome x new/returning/unknown visitor, with hits, distinct sessions, item steps, and (on session-total rows) bounced sessions. Each day has three row kinds: `Area = all` session totals (each session counted once that day), `ScopeType = all` per-area totals (a session counted once per area it touched), and per-area, per-scope rows (null scope means unscoped hits). Rollups always exclude bot hits and internal sessions. Outcome is judged on the session's hits that day. Channel, device, and country come from the session's attribution snapshot (`unknown` channel/device when no snapshot exists). A visitor is `new` when this is their earliest recorded session, `returning` when an earlier one exists, and `unknown` without a visitor id. The job runs in process as `PageViewRetentionService`, a `BackgroundService` like `StudyGuideCleanupService`, which works because the API is hosted as a long-running process. Reports switch by endpoint: raw-hit reports (Pages and Sessions tabs) are capped at the retention window, and longer ranges are served only from rollups by a separate Trends report.

- **AC 5.9.1** [System] **Given** the API starts, and every 24 hours after that, **then** a maintenance pass (1) classifies unclassified hits (AC 5.8.2), (2) rolls up every complete UTC day after the latest rolled-up day (or from the earliest hit when none exists) through yesterday, replacing that day's rows in one transaction, so re-running a day never duplicates rows, and (3) deletes `PageViews` older than 180 days and orphaned `AnalyticsSessions` older than the same cutoff. The prune cutoff is never later than the start of today, so a hit is never deleted before its day is rolled up. A failed pass is logged and prunes nothing.
- **AC 5.9.2** [Admin] **Behavior change —** `GET /admin/page-view-analytics` and `GET /admin/page-view-sessions` accept `days` from 1 to **180** (was 365). Larger values return **400** with a message pointing to the rollup report.
- **AC 5.9.3** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/page-view-rollups` with optional `days` (1–730, default 365), **then** the API returns the window (complete UTC days ending yesterday), the retention period, the latest rolled-up day, totals (hits, session-days, bounced session-days, item steps), a daily series (sessions, hits, item steps), per-area hits, session-days, and item steps from the per-area total rows (area hits add up to total hits), session-days and hits by channel, device class, outcome, and visitor type, and the top 25 categories/collections by hits. `days` outside 1–730 returns **400**.
- **AC 5.9.4** [Admin] **Given** I open **Usage Analytics → Trends**, **then** I can choose 90 days, 180 days, 1 year (default), or 2 years, and I see total cards, a daily sessions bar chart, a feature-area table, and channel, device, outcome, and new-vs-returning lists. The tab explains that bots and internal traffic are always excluded and that the page-level time range and toggles do not apply.
- **AC 5.9.5** [Anyone] **Given** I read the Privacy Policy, **then** its Retention section states that page-view records are deleted after 180 days, that traffic-attribution records are deleted once older than 180 days with no page views left, that daily usage totals without identifiers may be kept longer, and that error reports are deleted after 90 days.

### AC 5.10 Admin overview and daily trends (ANALYTICS-005 #125)

- **AC 5.10.1** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/analytics/overview` with `days` of 7, 30, or 90 (default 7), **then** the API returns, for the window of whole UTC days ending today and for the previous window of equal length: human sessions, engaged sessions, study sessions, converted sessions, new signups (`Users.CreatedAt`, internal users excluded), active registered users, and new and returning visitors. Session numbers use the same classification as the Sessions and Acquisition reports. Bots and internal sessions are always excluded. Any other `days` returns **400**; a non-admin caller gets **403**.
- **AC 5.10.2** [Admin] **Then** the response includes a daily series with exactly one point per UTC day of the window (zero-filled), release markers (the first UTC day each app version appeared in non-internal, non-bot traffic, only when that day is inside the window), and work-queue counts for items awaiting review, pending private keywords, and ideas awaiting moderation.
- **AC 5.10.3** [Admin] **Given** I open `/admin`, **then** above the tool grid I see KPI cards with the value and percentage change versus the previous window (the card shows "new" instead of a percentage when the previous value is zero), a plain-SVG daily chart with release markers (versions first seen on the same day share one label; a label that would overlap the previous one moves to a second or third row; a label near the right edge is right-aligned so it is never clipped; the chart's accessible name lists every version and day), a 7/30/90 day selector, and work-queue counts that link to the review board, private keyword review, ideas moderation, and Client Errors pages (the last is the number of front-end error groups first seen in the window, excluding bot and internal traffic, as in AC 5.6.6; `workQueue.newClientErrorGroups` in the API). Loading, empty ("No traffic recorded"), and error states are shown.

### AC 5.11 Admin feedback inbox (ANALYTICS-006 #126)

- **AC 5.11.1** **Given** I submit the feedback form, **then** the submission starts with status `New` and stores the current analytics session id (optional, at most 128 characters) so an admin can see the journey that led to the report.
- **AC 5.11.2** [Admin] **Given** I call `GET /admin/feedback`, **then** submissions are returned newest first, paged (`page`, `pageSize` 1-100), filterable by `type`, `status` (New, Reviewed, Archived), `from`/`to` UTC dates (inclusive), and `search` over details, page URL, and emails; the response includes the total and the count of `New` submissions. An invalid status or paging value returns **400**. `GET /admin/feedback/{id}` returns one submission (**404** when missing).
- **AC 5.11.3** [Admin] **Given** I call `PUT /admin/feedback/{id}/status` with `New`, `Reviewed`, or `Archived`, **then** the status persists; any other value returns **400** and an unknown id **404**.
- **AC 5.11.4** Every admin feedback endpoint returns **403** for non-admins (and **401** when unauthenticated). Contact emails (signed-in user email and the optional email typed into the form) are returned only by these endpoints.
- **AC 5.11.5** [Admin] **Given** I open `/admin/feedback`, **then** I see submissions with type, status, details, page URL, user and contact email, user agent, and time; I can filter by type, status (default New), date range, and text search; mark a submission New, Reviewed, or Archived; and show the session journey when a session id exists. The `/admin` dashboard shows the count of `New` submissions on the Feedback card. Loading, empty, and error states are shown.

### AC 5.12 Admin: AI providers (#202)

**API**

- **AC 5.12.1** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/llm-providers`, **then** the API returns 200 with `vendors` (DeepSeek, OpenAI, Gemini, each with `vendor`, `keyConfigured`, and `model`), `routes` (`free` and `premium`: ordered vendor names), `monthlySectionBudget` (config `StudyGuideGeneration:MonthlySectionBudget`, default 1000), and `monthlySectionsUsed` (section generations queued this UTC month, AC 6.6.10). On first read the settings are seeded with models DeepSeek `deepseek-flash`, OpenAI `gpt-5-nano`, Gemini `gemini-2.5-flash-lite`, free route Gemini → OpenAI → DeepSeek, and premium route DeepSeek → OpenAI → Gemini.
- **AC 5.12.2** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `PUT /admin/llm-providers` with `{ vendors: [{ vendor, model }], routes: { free, premium } }`, **then** the API validates it (400 with validation errors otherwise): each vendor is a known vendor (case-insensitive) listed at most once; each model is 1–100 characters matching `^[A-Za-z0-9._:\-/]+$`; each route lists 1–3 distinct known vendors. Valid input updates the listed vendors' models and both routes and returns 200 with the same shape as GET; vendors not listed keep their model.
- **AC 5.12.3** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/llm-providers/attempts?days=N` (`days` 1–90, default 7), **then** the API returns `{ attempts: [{ createdUtc, vendor, model, outcome, inputTokens, outputTokens, durationMs }] }`, newest first, at most 200 rows; `outcome` is `Success`, `ProviderError`, `Timeout`, `ContentFilter`, `LowQuoteYield`, `InvalidJson`, or `AuthError` (the vendor rejected our key: HTTP 401; HTTP 403 whose body mentions an API key, authentication or credentials; or HTTP 400 with "API key" in the body; a 403 without such wording, for example a region or model-access block, is a `ProviderError`, and rows recorded before this rule may show `AuthError` for any 403). Each row also carries `httpStatus` (the vendor's HTTP status for every reply that was received, including 200 replies that turn out unusable; null for a timeout, a network failure, a missing key, or rows recorded before this field existed).
- **AC 5.12.4** [Authenticated (non-admin) / Anonymous] Every `/admin/llm-providers*` endpoint returns 403 for authenticated non-admins and 401 for anonymous callers.
- **AC 5.12.5** [System] Vendor API keys come only from configuration / environment (`DeepSeek:ApiKey` or `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`, `OpenAI:ApiKey` or `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `Gemini:ApiKey` or `GEMINI_API_KEY`); they are never stored in the database, logged (the `Authorization` header is redacted from HTTP logs), or returned — the API only reports `keyConfigured`. The body of an HTTP 401 reply and of a 400 `AuthError` reply is never logged (only the vendor and HTTP status; OpenAI and DeepSeek echo a masked key fragment there). An HTTP 403 reply (whether classified `AuthError` or `ProviderError`) and every other failed reply log a single-line excerpt of at most 300 characters. Key-shaped text is redacted from the whole body before the excerpt is cut, and replaced with `[redacted]`. The field or header name, its `:` or `=` separator and any surrounding quotes are kept. The following are redacted:
  - `sk-…` keys (including `sk-proj-…`) at the start of a word;
  - `sk-…` stuck to a preceding letter or digit, when at least 20 key characters follow and one of the first 48 is a digit;
  - `sk_live_…`, `sk_test_…`, `rk_live_…` and `rk_test_…` keys;
  - `AIza…`;
  - runs of `*` followed by 3–6 letters or digits;
  - Bearer tokens (subject to the token-shape rule below);
  - the value of an `Authorization`, `x-goog-api-key`, `x-api-key`, `api-key` / `api_key` / `apikey` / `api key` or `access_token` / `access-token` / `accessToken` header or bare `name: value` / `name=value` pair, with or without a `Bearer`, `Basic`, `Token`, `Digest` or `ApiKey` scheme;
  - the values of the `api-key`, `api_key`, `apikey`, `x-api-key`, `x-goog-api-key`, `key`, `token`, `access_token`, `authorization`, `secret` and `password` JSON fields, whether double-quoted (including escaped quotes inside the value), single-quoted or a number, and the same fields when the JSON is echoed inside an escaped string;
  - a field string value that the vendor cut off before its closing quote, up to the end of the body;
  - the values of the `key`, `api_key`, `apikey`, `token` and `access_token` URL query parameters (including HTML-encoded `&amp;key=`).
  - Token-shape rule: so that ordinary error text stays readable, a Bearer token, and a header or bare value that follows `:` without quotes, are redacted only when they look like a token: 16 or more characters, or a digit within the first 15 characters, or a symbol inside the value, or a lowercase letter followed by an uppercase letter. For example, "Bearer token expired" and "authorization: required for this endpoint" are logged unchanged. A value after `=`, or a quoted value (up to its closing quote, including spaces and escaped quotes), is always redacted.
  - Known limits (not redacted): JSON object or array values of secret fields, a short letters-only value after an unquoted `api_key:`, mixed quotes such as `{'api_key': "x"}`, bare `password=` / `secret:` / `token=`, and unknown schemes such as `Authorization: Key <token>`. Known over-redaction: prose such as "authorization: 3 attempts", "API key: 2 keys max", "Bearer 2fa required", "authorization: re-authenticate" (a symbol inside a word) or a 16+ letter word after `authorization:`; `disk_test_validation`-style text (`sk_test_` / `rk_live_` shapes with no word boundary); and an unterminated quote after a header name, which redacts to the end of the body.
- **AC 5.12.6** [Admin] **Given** I am an admin, **when** I call `GET /admin/llm-providers/usage?days=N` (`days` 1–90, default 30; outside that range or not an integer → 400, unlike the attempts endpoint which clamps), **then** the API returns 200 `{ days, sinceUtc, rows, topUsers }`. `rows` has one entry per (vendor, model) with at least one `LlmAttempts` row created since `sinceUtc`, ordered by vendor then model (ordinal, case-sensitive): `{ vendor, model, attempts, successes, inputTokens, outputTokens, avgDurationMs, estimatedCostUsd }`. `successes` counts `Success` outcomes; token sums include failed attempts (missing tokens count as 0) and are 64-bit; `avgDurationMs` is the rounded mean. `topUsers` lists up to 20 users, ordered by charged sections then total tokens (both descending), then user id ascending (ordinal, case-sensitive), from `StudyGuideQuotaCharges` created since `sinceUtc` (refunded charges no longer exist and do not count): `{ userId, chargedSections, inputTokens, outputTokens }`, user id only. `chargedSections` is the sum of charge costs; a user's tokens are the recorded tokens of the generations they were charged for, counted once per generation, from the kept attempt only (failed-vendor tokens appear only in `rows`; a generation not yet completed counts 0 tokens). Users who only attached to an existing generation for free are not listed. Attempts are kept for `AttemptRetentionDays` (default 90), which can shorten the effective window. [System] Totals are aggregated in the database: one grouped query over `LlmAttempts` returns one row per (vendor, model), and one grouped query over `StudyGuideQuotaCharges` (grouped per user and generation, left-joined to `SectionGenerations`, then grouped per user) returns one row per user. Individual attempt and charge rows are not loaded into the API. `avgDurationMs` is the summed duration divided by the attempt count, rounded midpoint-away-from-zero. Ordering, the top-20 cut and estimated cost are applied in the API after aggregation.
- **AC 5.12.7** [Admin] **Given** a model has prices configured as `LlmPricing:{model}:InputPerMTok` and `LlmPricing:{model}:OutputPerMTok` (USD per million tokens; put them in appsettings/JSON, since environment-variable keys cannot hold names with `-` and `.` comfortably), **then** `estimatedCostUsd` is `round(inputTokens / 1,000,000 × input price + outputTokens / 1,000,000 × output price, 4)` using decimal arithmetic, midpoints rounded away from zero. Both prices are required and must parse as non-negative invariant-culture decimals (only `.` as the decimal separator); otherwise `estimatedCostUsd` is null. A model name containing `:` can never be priced. No prices ship by default (`"LlmPricing": {}`).

**UI**

- **AC 5.12.10** [Admin] **Given** I am on the Admin Dashboard, **when** I click **AI Providers**, **then** I am taken to `/admin/ai-providers`; non-admins are redirected home.
- **AC 5.12.11** [Admin] **Given** the AI providers page loads, **then** I see the monthly section budget ("X of Y sections generated this month", with a labeled progress bar) and a vendors table listing DeepSeek, OpenAI, and Gemini with a text badge **Key configured** / **No key** (keys are never shown) and a labeled **Model for <vendor>** input.
- **AC 5.12.12** [Admin] **Given** the **Free route** and **Premium route** editors, **when** I reorder vendors, **then** each entry has labeled **Up** / **Down** / **Remove** buttons (keyboard-operable; Up is disabled on the first entry and Down on the last) and an **Add vendor to <route>** select for vendors not yet in the route; vendors without a key are marked "(no key, skipped)". When the saved route is non-empty and none of its vendors has a key, a `role="alert"` message under the route heading reads "No vendor in this route has a key; generation for free users is off." (or "premium users"); it follows the saved route only (unsaved edits do not toggle it) and updates after saving, and an empty route keeps the "No vendors in this route." line. **Save settings** validates with Zod (model 1–100 characters matching `^[A-Za-z0-9._:\-/]+$`, each route 1–3 distinct known vendors) and shows the first error (prefixed with the vendor for model errors); valid settings are sent with `PUT /admin/llm-providers` (models trimmed) and the page shows "AI provider settings saved."
- **AC 5.12.13** [Admin] **Given** the **Recent attempts** table, **when** I choose a window (last day, 7 days, 30 days), **then** it lists `GET /admin/llm-providers/attempts?days=N` rows newest first with time, vendor, model, outcome as text ("Auth error (check key)" for `AuthError`; unknown values are shown as-is), an **HTTP** status column ("—" when none), input / output tokens, and duration.
- **AC 5.12.14** [Admin] **Given** the AI providers page, **then** below **Recent attempts** a **Usage** section (admin only) has a labeled **Usage window** select (Last day, Last 7 days, Last 30 days — the default, Last 90 days) and loads `GET /admin/llm-providers/usage?days=N` (AC 5.12.6) on every change without flashing the empty state. A "By vendor and model" table shows Vendor, Model, Attempts, Success rate (rounded %), Tokens in / out, Avg duration (ms), and Est. cost (`$x.xxxx`, or "n/a" with the tooltip "No price configured for this model"). A "Top users" table (note: "Top 20 users by charged sections in this window.") shows User ID, Charged sections, and Tokens in / out, or "No charged sections in this window." when empty. While loading it shows a spinner; on failure "Failed to load usage" with a retry button; with no rows, "No vendor calls in this window." replaces both tables. Tables scroll horizontally inside their container on narrow screens.
- **AC 5.12.15** [Admin] **Given** I open `/admin/ai-providers` at a 375px-wide viewport, **then** the page itself does not scroll sideways (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= 375`). The Vendors, Recent attempts and Usage tables scroll inside their own containers, the Free and Premium route editors stack in one column, and each route entry wraps its Up / Down / Remove buttons onto their own line instead of overflowing.
